Just a caveat, though, they don’t do it for free. Depending on the fund management company, fees can be based on your investments’ market value or their performance for the year. Make sure to read the terms and conditions carefully and compare the fees of at least two different funds or trusts.

An example of a cryptocurrency investment fund is Grayscale. At the end of 2020, it was the biggest cryptocurrency fund in the world, with managed funds totaling over $10 billion in market value. Established in 2013, Grayscale initially focused on Bitcoin and was first called the Grayscale Bitcoin Investment Trust. Over the years, it has added more digital tokens to its portfolio and is now known simply as Grayscale to emphasize its newly diversified investment lineup.

When you invest in managed funds, how do you make money? Just as with directly investing in digital coins, you make money when your investments’ net asset value or NAV goes up. 

NAV pertains to the net book value of each share of a managed fund. How’s it different from market prices of assets like cryptocurrencies and publicly traded stocks? The net asset value is typically computed by adding the total market value of the securities in the portfolio and its cash assets less any liabilities. The net portfolio value is then divided by the total number of outstanding shares owned by investors.

As you can see, managed funds are still market-driven investments. However, their sheer size plus having professional fund managers on board ensure that your investments are well diversified, closely monitored, and properly managed. Unless you have all the time in the world, the relevant experiences and expertise, and millions of dollars of investable funds, it may be hard to enjoy the benefits of managed funds and trusts.

Another alternative cryptocurrency investment is derivatives, particularly futures contracts and options. What are these?

Both futures and options allow you to buy or sell assets, such as cryptocurrencies, at a fixed price in the future regardless of what the prevailing market prices are at that time. The only difference is that with futures contracts, you’re obligated to buy or sell a fixed number of units or shares of a particular asset on a specific future date at an agreed-upon price. 

On the other hand, options give you the right to buy or sell the same on or before a specific future date at a fixed price. Unlike with futures contracts, you may choose whether to exercise the option to buy or sell to your counterparty. Another difference between an options contract and a futures contract is that you can exercise your options to buy or sell within a time period instead of a specific date.

How can you make money with derivatives like these? Just like with any market-driven security, you buy contracts like these at a low price and sell at a higher one. In essence, you’re not buying cryptocurrencies themselves. Instead, you’re buying and selling contracts where digital coins are the underlying assets or subjects of the contract. 

![black and silver laptop computer](Images/image00325.jpeg)

This is how derivatives work. They’re financial instruments or contracts that get their values from those of the underlying assets. When the market prices of the underlying cryptocurrencies go up, the market price of call (buy) futures contracts and options go up, too. Because these give you opportunities to buy digital coins at a fixed price, you stand to earn more after selling the underlying assets at much higher prices in the future.

On the other hand, the values of put (sell) futures contracts and options tend to go down when the market prices of underlying digital coins go up. It’s because when the assets’ prices exceed your futures selling price at maturity, you’re obligated to sell to your buying counterparty at lower than the market. You may not necessarily suffer losses, but the opportunity loss may be great enough to make you scratch your head in frustration. 

When it comes to options, it won’t be as bad as with futures selling contracts when the prices of underlying cryptocurrencies go up. Sure, the value of a put or sell option decreases as the prices of underlying assets rise because who would want to exercise the option of selling at a lower than market price? 

Where can you buy and sell cryptocurrency futures and options? For futures contracts, they are available on the Chicago Mercantile Exchange or CME. For options, you may do so on the Chicago Board Options Exchange or CBOE. However, you can’t trade directly on these two platforms, so you’ll need to sign up for an account with its member brokers.

Among the alternative cryptocurrency investments, your best option depends on a lot of factors, including your risk tolerance, investing experience and skills, and personal preferences.

If you want the experience of potentially higher returns without having to deal with closely monitoring and managing this volatile type of investment, investing in managed funds may be your best bet. If you’re the DIY type of investor, investing directly in cryptocurrencies may be in order. And if you’re an adrenaline junkie and after high-stakes trading, you will probably find what you’re looking for by trading cryptocurrency derivatives.

## Chapter 7

 

## Choosing Your Trading Platform

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

After you have chosen the cryptocurrency you’d like to first invest in, the next thing you should do is look for the right platform to buy your first cryptocurrency and eventually trade (buy and sell) them. 

You can avoid many of the mistakes beginners typically make by establishing the criteria for choosing your platforms beforehand. Doing so can help you avoid the same fate a lot of newbie crypto investors suffer, which includes choosing a platform or exchange where their chosen cryptocurrencies are either not traded there, can’t be bought with fiat or regular currencies, or both. Setting your criteria beforehand also helps you invest/trade digital coins securely by choosing platforms that offer good security and aren’t as prone to hacking as others. 

Now that you know the importance of setting the right criteria for choosing your crypto trading/investing platform, what are they? These include the following, not necessarily in order of importance:

1.  Market reputation of the trading platform.
2.  Payment methods for both buying digital coins and getting the proceeds from selling them.
3.  Quality of customer support.
4.  Server and website safety and security.
5.  Supported currency pairs, both in terms of the number of pairs and kinds of pairs.
6.  Trading volume and liquidity, i.e., how easy can you liquidate your digital coins.
7.  Transparency in transactions, price, volume, and coins.
8.  Transactions costs, e.g., fees and spreads; and
9.  User-friendliness, especially for newbies.

Once you’ve selected the criteria for choosing your trading platform, it’s time to look at some of the best ones in more detail. The following are arranged alphabetically, and their order doesn’t mean one is superior to the others. Each of them has its own advantages and limitations, which you’ll evaluate in order to determine which one’s the most appropriate for you.

Coinbase

In the United States, Coinbase is considered the leading mainstream cryptocurrency trading platform. Particularly for beginner investors and traders, it has become the standard on-boarding trading platform. Some of the reasons for this include:

1.  A wide variety of digital coin investing products.
2.  It is a sophisticated trading platform.
3.  Institutional custodial accounts.
4.  Retail investor wallets. 
5.  Being fully regulated and licensed; and
6.  Having its very own stable coin that’s denominated in U.S. dollars.

The platform was founded back in 2012, and it supports digital coin trading all over the United States, except in Hawaii. At first, cryptocurrency trading on the platform was limited to just Bitcoin. But over time, it started to add more cryptocurrencies that were able to meet its criteria of decentralization. Eventually, its digital coin lineup expanded to include other cryptocurrency heavyweights like Bitcoin Cash, XRP, Litecoin, and Ethereum. Coinbase continues to add new cryptocurrencies that meet its requirements.

One of the key advantages of using Coinbase to start your cryptocurrency investing journey is variety. Apart from the granddaddy of all cryptocurrencies, Bitcoin, and major altcoins like the ones mentioned above, the platform offers more than 25 digital coins for trading, long-term investing, and even staking.

Another key benefit of starting off with Coinbase is its user-friendly interface. For many beginners, Coinbase is considered one of the easiest and simplest cryptocurrency platforms to start with. Many find the signing up and account opening processes to be very easy, making it possible to start buying digital coins in a matter of minutes. And part of its user-friendly interface is a learning program that even pays its users digital coins to encourage them to learn more about how this type of investment asset works.

Another good reason to consider starting off with Coinbase is very high liquidity. In layman’s terms, it means it’s relatively easy to sell your cryptocurrency holdings in the platform when you need cash. This is because being one of the top digital coin trading platforms in the world, the average daily volume of transactions is very high. With high trading volume comes an easier time liquidating your investments.

High liquidity brings with it another benefit: relatively lower volatility. Keep in mind I do not mean low volatility, but compared to the market prices of digital coins in platforms with low liquidity or average daily trading volumes, the price swings in highly liquid platforms like Coinbase aren’t as wide. 

This is because higher trading volume means significantly more buyers and sellers competing against each other to get their transactions done. In platforms with significantly fewer buyers and sellers, competition is much lower.

With cutthroat competition, the price difference between bids and offers can be very narrow. With very small price gaps, changes in marketing value tend to be smaller. On the other hand, very little competition results in relatively bigger differences between the highest bid price and the lowest asking price for cryptocurrencies. That often results in bigger price movements.

However, Coinbase isn’t a perfect platform. It has its own share of disadvantages too.

One of these is relatively higher fees compared to its major competitors. As a beginner, this is something you might want to consider before getting on the platform. But there is a lower-cost alternative: Coinbase Pro.

Switching to Coinbase Pro is completely free, and more importantly, you can enjoy lower fees in the whole lot more interface features. But there is a catch: this version of the platform has significantly more features to work with that can be overwhelming for digital coin investors such as you.

Another disadvantage associated with trading on the Coinbase platform is having no control over your wallet keys, which is standard among centralized exchanges. Every time you buy cryptocurrencies on platforms such as this, they are initially stored in your Coinbase wallet that Coinbase has complete control over.

This turns off many investors because this runs counter to the decentralized nature of digital coins. Because it’s a fully regulated and licensed exchange, the platform is centralized.

But just like the high transaction fees of the non-pro version of Coinbase, there’s a workaround for this, too: using your own personal wallet. As soon as you’re done buying your digital coins, take them out of your platform wallet and store them on your own outside of the exchange. 

For maximum security of your digital coins, I highly recommend going for a hard cold storage wallet. As explained, unless you’re going to move your cryptos in and out of it, it is always offline. Hackers only have a crack at your investments if your wallet is perennially online. Thus, cold storage wallets are your best option.

The last disadvantage of trading on the Coinbase platform is the lack of altcoin variety. As a newbie investor, this may not necessarily be a disadvantage. At this stage in the game, having fewer options outside of Bitcoin minimize this your risks of being overwhelmed and suffering from analysis paralysis.

But from a long-term perspective, having a fewer number of available altcoins may limit your investment options in the future when you’ve already become adept at cryptocurrency investing. If you need more options, later, you’ll likely need to open an account in another trading platform with a significantly greater number of traded altcoins.

Fortunately, you can expect Coinbase to feature a greater number of altcoins by the time you’ve become a cryptocurrency trading ninja. It’s because, just as in the past, the platform is always looking to add new altcoins to its roster. 

Speaking of available points on the platform, its current offerings include the following:

As a beginner, trading on Coinbase can be an easy and enjoyable experience. One of the top reasons is accessibility. Because it’s a web-based platform that doesn’t require downloading and installing any software or program, you can easily access your account and trade online whether you’re using an Android phone, an iPhone, or your computer. The same holds true for its more advanced version, Coinbase Pro.

As soon as you log onto your account, you can easily see your updated portfolio balance and performance within your selected timeframe, such as the last hour, the last 24 hours, and even the last 12 months. You’ll also find a button labeled “Buy Crypto” in a banner positioned on top of your portfolio balance on the screen. By clicking on it, you can place you’re buying order for the specific digital coin you want to invest in. And while the button says “buy,” clicking on it also shows you options for selling your cryptocurrencies or converting them to other ones. Even better, you can also use the button to set up orders that happen on a regular basis, whether it’s daily, weekly, or on specific days of the month.

To buy your first cryptocurrency, just go to the upper right-hand corner of the web page, where you will find options for sending and receiving digital coins to and from your personal wallets using your Coinbase wallet. Again, this is a crucial feature to ensure the safety of your digital coin investments. Regardless of how secure platforms like this are, there’s always a risk of getting hacked, albeit a small one. While it’s true that successful hacks on trading platforms are considered as Black Swans, its impact on your investments will be huge when that low probability materializes, considering cryptocurrencies are far beyond the reach of government regulatory bodies.

Most other exchanges allow you to withdraw your tokens to your own wallet, including this platform. However, there are still some exchanges that do not allow this. That’s why if you’re going for another platform, make sure that it’ll give you the option to move your coins from your Coinbase wallet to your hard storage ones and vice versa.

Coinbase Pro

As mentioned earlier, Coinbase Pro is the more advanced version of the trading platform that offers you a lot more user interface features and lower transaction fees. While the basic version is an excellent onboarding platform for beginners, the pro version offers you a more robust trading experience. Some of the additional features available on the free upgrade include automated orders (market, stop-loss, limit), margin trading, and more advanced price charts that can make your trading more efficient and profitable. And as mentioned earlier, upgrading to it is free.

Speaking of more advanced price charts, Coinbase Pro can show charts that are as detailed as one-minute line and candle charts. If you eventually become a day trader, you’ll need these kinds of minutely detailed charts.

Another advanced charting feature available to Coinbase Pro account holders is the ability to use chart overlaid technical indicators like exponential moving averages. However, currently available options are limited to just two exponential moving average or EMA periods: 12 and 26. But this shouldn’t be a major limitation because these are the two most popularly used EMA periods among cryptocurrency traders and investors.

Another upgraded feature of the Pro account is extensive currency trading pairs. When you upgrade, you’ll have access to more than 100 trading pairs, with close to 40 of them involving the U.S. dollar and more than 40 crypto-crypto trading pairs available in 42 regions around the world.

Given its relatively young age, the technical indicators available on Coinbase pro are still simple compared to other older trading platforms. Given Coinbase’s track record of excellence and continuous improvements, the pro version will likely increase the number of its available technical analysis tools moving forward.

Coinbase Pro is an excellent and efficient way to evolve your cryptocurrency investing activities when you’ve outgrown the beginner stage. With the free upgrade, lower fees, and more robust features, this may be the next logical step to take if you choose to start investing in digital coins with the basic Coinbase platform.

Fees

How much would it cost you to use this platform? For the basic version, keep in mind that its fee structure can be highly convoluted aside from being expensive compared to its competitors. If you prioritize ease of trading platform use, consider the relatively expensive transaction costs as a tradeoff to enjoy such a benefit. 

Coinbase fees are different depending on the country and region. But aside from the standard transaction fees, the basic version of the platform also requires you to pay different spreads on your trades and purchases.

According to Coinbase, you are charged 0.5% of the transaction value of your purchases and sales of digital coins if they are worth less than $10,000. The higher the transaction values are, the spreads charged to you become lower. 

For example, you only pay 0.35% of your cryptocurrency transaction value as a spread fee if the amount is between $10,000 and $50,000. Maker-taker fees can go as low as 0.00% and 0.04%, respectively, once your transaction value exceeds $500 million. As a result, market price movements can play a huge role in determining how much spread fees you’ll pay.

But wait, there’s more! You’ll have to shoulder two other fees when you trade using the basic version of the Coinbase platform. These are deposit and Coinbase fees. The latter charge depends on factors such as your purchase transaction values, payment type, and the area or location where you’re using Coinbase to purchase cryptocurrencies.

As mentioned earlier, upgrading to a Coinbase Pro account entitles you to lower and simpler fees. Deposits and withdrawals of ACH transfers and digital assets are completely free, while depositing and withdrawing wire transfers will only set you back $10 and $25, respectively. 

Platform Security

To keep your accounts and cryptocurrencies safe, the platform employs different safety measures. However, this isn’t a guarantee that your digital investments are hacking-free. You’ll also have to do your part because any account maintained on a cryptocurrency exchange also depends greatly on the account safety practices of its owner. Even if your account is on the world’s most secure platform, it can still be hacked if you’re not careful about how you set and store your passwords. Hence, you must ensure your passwords are very strong, and you should maximize the platform’s security features like the two-step verification process.

Aside from the two-step verification feature, Coinbase employs other measures to maximize the security of your cryptocurrency investments. These include the use of biometric fingerprint logins, insurance on your account in the event it’s hacked due to a breach in the Coinbase platform, and storing up to 98% of its users’ funds in an offline cold storage facility.

Also, keep in mind that because cryptocurrencies don’t count as legal tender, they are not insured by the FDIC or SIPC. Instead, Coinbase ensures your funds and those of other users by pooling all your balances and keeping them in U.S. dollar-denominated custodial accounts, short-term financial securities, and/or U.S. treasuries.

But all things considered, the platform’s security measures are superior to many other top exchanges. As such, just do your part to secure your account, and you can rest assured that your digital assets will be safe. These include using very strong passwords, maximizing the platform’s security features, and using a cold storage wallet to store your digital coins.

Opening Your Coinbase Cryptocurrency Exchange Account

There’s a reason why many cryptocurrency investing professionals highly recommend Coinbase for investing newbies: An easy and straightforward account opening process. The steps include:

1.  Providing your complete name and email address.
2.  Setting your account password.
3.  Verify your email address.
4.  Provide your mobile phone number where the platform will send you on account a verification code. 
5.  Enter the verification code; and
6.  Enter your identification details.

As with any investment or bank account you open with other financial institutions, you’ll need a state identification to establish your legal identity. Here in the United States, you will need to provide a Social Security number. By now, you would have finished creating your Coinbase account and are already able to include your debit/credit card and bank account information. These will enable you to start depositing funds into your account so you can already begin investing in digital coins.

Coinbase Conclusion

Overall, Coinbase may be the ideal cryptocurrency trading platform for you as a beginner with very little or no investment experience. While the fees charged on its basic platform are more expensive compared to its other competitors, its robust features make it worth the cost. These include its learning program, setting up recurring transactions, simple, user-friendly interface, and the free option to upgrade to a Coinbase Pro account later when you become adept at cryptocurrency investing.

Kraken

![](Images/image00326.gif)

Based in San Francisco’s Silicon Valley, Kraken is another top cryptocurrency exchange platform that lets you buy and sell many digital coins using various fiat currencies such as the U.S. dollar, euros, and Japanese yen. Some of the most popular cryptos that are on this platform include established ones like Bitcoin (XBT) and Ethereum (ETH), as well as newer ones that are growing in popularity, such as Monero (XMR) and EOS (EOS). The platform is owned and managed by Payward, Inc., led by its co-founder and CEO Jesse Powell.

Established in 2011, Kraken officially started its trading operations in 2013. The platform provides easy money transfers to and from bank accounts linked to its users’ Kraken accounts and the easy movement of digital coins to and from their personal storage wallet and Kraken accounts.

Many factors contributed to making Kraken one of the most popular crypto trading platforms today, starting in 2014.

During that time, the cryptocurrency frenzy was just starting the spread all over the world thanks to Bitcoin. As a result, a growing number of market data providers suddenly wanted to include Bitcoin in their rosters. One of them was Bloomberg.

Fortunately for Kraken, Bloomberg chose to partner with it and Coinbase for its Bitcoin market data requirements of its myriad number of client terminals all around the world. Because Kraken was pivotal in giving Bloomberg clients direct access to real-time and historical information on Bitcoin and altcoins, it quickly became a household name in many financial communities. And considering that Bloomberg is one of the world’s top financial data and information providers, doing business with it provided Kraken the validation and status in the international trading community that helped catapult it to rock star status.

It wasn’t long after that when Kraken decided to team up with TradingView, the world’s top in-chart service provider. Afterward, the platform continued to expand its cryptocurrency lineup and added multi-fiat currency funding and trading, e.g., U.S. Dollar, British Pound, and Japanese Yen. 

After launching its initial phase, Kraken started to become even more popular towards the end of its stellar 2014, thanks largely to the infamous Mt. Gox hacking incident. At that time, Mt. Gox was the biggest cryptocurrency exchange in the world. Its status in the cryptocurrency industry and the skyrocketing interest and awareness of digital coins combined, made the hacking incident that caused so many of its users to lose their investments stored in the platform, an event worthy of being on the headline of many major news outlets. 

How did this benefit Kraken? It was chosen to help in finding the stolen/missing Bitcoins that hackers made away with. It was also tapped to assist in distributing Mt. Gox’s remaining assets to its creditors. 

Because Mt. Gox creditors (mostly account owners who lost their investments in the hacking incident) were required to open accounts with Kraken to facilitate their claims, the number of users on the platform suddenly grew by leaps and bounds. With a substantial number of the “inherited” accounts choosing to stay and trade with Kraken even after it started approving Mt. Gox creditor claims, its daily trading volume quickly ballooned as well. As a result, it became one of the tops – if not the top – cryptocurrency platforms in the world almost overnight. 

With two very high-profile developments in one year, Kraken’s market standing catapulted from just being one of the many available crypto-trading platforms to that of rock star status. Prior to these two major developments, Kraken had just started in Japan and was focusing mainly on European customers through a collaboration with a leading German bank. 

Two factors contributed to it being chosen by Bloomberg and the authorities that were on top of the Mt. Gox post-hacking incident activities: its relatively conservative approach and regulatory compliance. 

Over the years, its popularity continued to grow, due largely in part to the introduction of new trading platform features in 2015 that made cryptocurrency investing widely popular not just in investing communities but even among the masses. Two of these features were margin trading facilities and dark pool services. 

Margin trading facilities are important for both exchanges and their expert customers. When you have access to a margin account, you can buy more cryptocurrencies than your current cash balance can afford. Essentially, it’s a trading credit facility extended by platforms like Kraken to its customers so they can increase their transaction volumes and, ultimately, revenues from transaction fees.

As a customer or investor, access to a margin account can help you maximize your potential crypto trading income. Imagine this: if you can earn a $500 trading income using your $5000 current exchange account balance, you can double it to $1000 by using a margin account that can let you trade by as much as two times your balance.

However, there’s a reason why margin trading accounts aren’t recommended for beginners. It can be a double-edged sword; it doesn’t just double your potential trading gains but also doubles your losses. Because it’s very easy to spend money that you don’t have, the temptation to easily double your position on very risky cryptos can be very strong. If the price of the coins you bought on margin suddenly plunges, you can very quickly lose so much money and be compelled by the platform to put in more money in your account via a margin call. 

Dark pool services allow you to get trades done at potentially more favorable prices compared to what’s currently in the market. Through such services, you can place relatively large orders in a very discreet way that’s matched against other similar-sized ones. This is how you may get opportunities to trade at highly beneficial prices.

Kraken has been able to maintain – and even grow – the huge market share it gained in 2014 by consistently evolving its trading platform’s features that cater to both retail and institutional customers. It also offers a diverse range of accounts for a wide variety of investors, from high-risk tolerance traders to conservative long-term position takers.

The exchange also features an advanced order-taking system and technical trading tools. Aside from margin trading and dark pool services, Kraken’s platform also features programmable stop-loss and profit-taking orders and automated strategy investing. And with a wide selection of digital coins available for trading, it’s not surprising to find Kraken among the world’s top cryptocurrency trading platforms. 

Just like with Coinbase, Kraken is an excellent exchange that has its share of imperfections. One of the most glaring issues with the platform is network security.

In May 2017, Kraken came under serious DDoS (Distributed Denial of Service) attacks that caused many of its customers to lose a lot of money. A website or a platform is overloaded with too many requests on its I.P. address during a DDoS. This results in clogging of its network or server, which prevents others from getting in. It’s like watching a BTS concert in a 100-seater cafe: the place is ultra-jam-packed, that a lot of patrons are denied entrance to the establishment even if they have tickets. 

Because of the attacks, Kraken was hit with a class-action suit. The plaintiffs were their customers who lost money because they were unable to execute timely trades as a result of the platform becoming inaccessible. They sought $5 million in damages that they claimed resulted from Kraken’s failure to manage the DDoS attack effectively. As you can imagine, the incident struck a big blow to the platform’s reputation as the safest cryptocurrency exchange. 

Another issue facing the platform concerns is its scalability and stability. With the renewed spike in Bitcoin market prices followed by skyrocketing values of Dogecoin, the cryptocurrency frenzy continues to set the investing world on fire. As a result, multitudes of new investors continue to flood the digital coins market, hoping to hop on the modern-day version of the Holland Tulip craze, the California Gold Rush, and the Dot Com mania.

As a result, it’s not unusual to regularly hear of several exchanges experiencing slower trading processes, which can negatively impact trading volume and liquidity. Kraken wasn’t spared from this when it registered over 50,000 new trading accounts coupled with over 10,000 new support tickets in a period of several days. The latter is a clear indication that many of its customers encountered technical issues on Kraken in just a few short days. 

Kraken Conclusion

The platform continues to enjoy its status as one of the world’s biggest and most popular digital coin trading exchanges. While it has had its share of technical issues, these were “minor” and didn’t result in the theft of customers’ digital coins and funds, unlike what happened to other prominent exchanges like Mt. Gox. Its robust trading features like margin trading and dark pool services make it a more appropriate platform for experienced investors. 

Binance

Founded in 2017, Binance is a relatively young crypto trading platform that focuses more on altcoin trading. Its huge array of tradeable coins (over 500 cryptocurrencies and virtual tokens) ensure it continues to grow in popularity among non-U.S. investors. However, only about 1/10 or over 50 coins and tokens are accessible for investors in the United States. Nevertheless, these include many of the most established cryptos like Bitcoin, Ethereum, Litecoin, Dogecoin, and its proprietary token, Binance Coin. 

Despite being one of the youngest cryptocurrency exchange platforms in the market, Binance’s rise in popularity has been nothing short of exceptional. Some of the factors that contributed to this include:

1.  Low transaction fees.
2.  High average daily trading volume and liquidity.
3.  Customer incentives, like extra discounts, for using its very own B&B tokens for payments.

Another unique characteristic of this exchange is its focus on crypto-to-crypto trading. This means cryptocurrencies are bought using other tokens instead of fiat currencies like the U.S. dollar or the Japanese yen.

In case you’re wondering, the platform’s name was based on two very popular words: Bitcoin and finance. As an exchange, it boasts of high safety and security standards that feature multi clustered and multi-tiered structures. In simple terms, its network has many layers of protection, making it highly impervious to hacks.

It also boasts of very fast processing times that can process as much as 1.4 million orders every second. With such a capacity, scalability and stability isn’t an issue for the platform. 

As expected with any exchange, Binance allows you to buy and sell cryptocurrencies on the platform. You can do this either by using other digital coins or fiat currencies. And if you ever get to the point that you’d like to launch your own coins, you can use the platform to raise funds via initial coin offerings or ICOs. 

To start buying and selling on Binance, you need to fill up and submit KYC-related documents. After you’ve successfully opened an account on the exchange, you’ll be given your own public wallet address where you can transfer funds for buying cryptocurrencies, and where proceeds of tokens that you’ll sell later will be credited.

On Binance, you can execute three kinds of trading orders. These are limit orders, market orders, and stop-limit orders. Limit orders pertain to transactions that are executed at the limit prices you set. 

When you post a market order, the system will immediately execute the transaction at the prevailing relevant market price. It will execute the transaction at the immediate or lowest asking price if it’s a purchase order. If it’s a sell transaction, the platform will execute your order immediately at the highest bid price.

The system will execute a transaction only when current market prices reach your specified levels for stop limit orders. Also referred to as stop-loss orders, the system will automatically liquidate your position once your marked-to-market losses reach your set limit based on market movements.

One of the coolest things about Binance is that your choices aren’t limited to cryptocurrencies alone. You can also trade cryptocurrency derivatives, particularly futures contracts. Aside from the three kinds of orders mentioned earlier, you have five other transaction options for crypto derivatives: stock market, trailing stop, post only, limit T.P. and limit S.L. orders.

When you deposit additional funds or cryptocurrencies in your Binance account, you don’t have to pay any fees. But for withdrawals of the same, there are transaction fees. Amounts will vary according to the digital token involved and transaction value.

Currently, you can fund your investments by depositing U.S. dollars, Euros, and eleven other currencies to your account using the SWIFT network. If you don’t have access to said network, you have another option. You can buy digital coins on the platform through your debit or credit card.

Why should you consider investing using the Binance trading platform? Aside from access to exchange-specific services, you can also access other services, platforms, and tools for supporting an entire blockchain ecosystem. It operates a blockchain technology incubator that goes by the name Binance Labs. 

As you may have guessed, its primary goal is to support pre-ICO stage projects that have great potential. Through Binance Labs, the company provides crucial financial support to teams of deserving cryptocurrency projects to fund the development of the planned coins, acquire the necessary resources, and obtain all necessary requirements for launching their planned tokens.

Aside from the lab, Binance also has a website named launchpad. The platform is used for hosting up-and-coming blockchain projects and API interfaces. Today, the website has helped launch 42 projects already.

Binance Coin (BNB)

In July 2017, the company embarked on its initial coin offering. It offered 10%, 40%, and the remaining 50% of the offered tokens to angel investors, its founding team, and all other parties involved in the ICO, respectively. The number of units offered in the ICO totaled 200 million BNB.

Of the ICO proceeds, almost half of the proceeds are dedicated to branding and marketing operations, and about 1/3 was used for creating or building the trading platform and making necessary upgrades to its ecosystem.

BNB uses the Ethereum blockchain, specifically the ERC 20 standard, with a cap of 200 million tokens. As of the first quarter of 2021, a unit of Binance Coin is trading at $650. At this level, BNB has the third-highest market capitalization at around $3.6 billion. 

Binance Conclusion

As a cryptocurrency trading platform, it supports many of today’s most popular digital coins. It also provides its customers with their own crypto wallet on the exchange for storing their electronic funds. If you need help making investment decisions, you may also find support programs helpful.

Compared to the other platforms, it has particularly important features if you plan to launch your own token later. But even if you don’t have such plans, it’s still something worth considering because it can positively impact the long-term prices of its digital token, the Binance Coin. 

But as a beginning investor, you’d be better off with simpler and more user-friendly interfaces like those of Coinbase’s. Most of the features of this platform cater to more experienced cryptocurrency investors and those who have plans of launching their own coins via an ICO. 

Gemini

Founded in 2014 in the U.S. by the famed Winklevoss twins - remember the origins of Facebook? - Gemini is a private and licensed platform for digital trading assets that provides digital holding custodian services since its founding; the exchange has widened its net to include Europe and Asia. 

The platform was officially launched for customers in the United States in October 2015. Since then, it has grown to become one of the top 15 crypto platforms in terms of trading size per the data and analytics firm CoinMarketCap. And compared to other exchanges, it’s available in all 50 states in the U.S., on top of being accessible to 50 other countries. 

Key Features

One of the best features of this trading platform, especially for those like you who are relatively new to this investment genre, is its simplicity. And if you’re already a seasoned trader, you will like Gemini’s ActiveTrader facilities. 

Currently, you can choose from 33 offered digital coins if you plan to trade using U.S. dollars. These include the usual heavyweights like Bitcoin, Litecoin, and Ethereum. 

The platform implements minimum trading volume requirements, which vary according to the cryptocurrency being transacted. For example, the minimum number of units you should transact for Bitcoin is 0.00001 unit and 0.001 unit for Ethereum.

When it comes to transaction fees, it’s not one of the cheapest in the market. However, its high-level security measures coupled with insurance for your account against online breaches on the platform make the extra cost well worth it. Would you prefer dirt-low transaction fees at the expense of security and safety? I doubt it.

Speaking of transaction fees, they can go as high as 1.49% of the transaction value depending on the Gemini trading platform you’ll use. If you purchase digital coins using your debit card, fees go up further to 3.49% of the transaction value. You may also have to pay other fees as they apply.

Gemini charges a basic convenience fee equivalent to 0.5% of the current trading price of the digital coins you’re trading. Aside from that, you’ll also pay a tiered flat fee for transactions of up to $200. Higher than that, the additional fee is 1.49% of the transaction amount. 

To give you a clearer picture of trading costs on this exchange, here’s a table showing tiered fees in dollar terms according to transaction size:

Transaction Amount Transaction Fees in $ Amount

Up to $10 $0.99

Between $10 and $25 - $1.49

Between $25 and $50 - $1.99

Between $50 and $200 - $2.99

Higher than $200 - 1.49% of your Web order’s transaction amount

One benefit of choosing Gemini over Coinbase is you won’t pay fees to the exchange for deposits coming from wire transfers or your U.S. bank accounts. Your bank, however, may levy you with transfer fees. 

Another benefit is lower fees for cryptocurrency purchases funded by debit cards. While you’ll pay a significantly higher fee of 3.49% of transaction value compared to a maximum of 1.49% if you used the funds already in your Gemini account, it’s still cheaper compared to the same option on the Coinbase and Binance platforms that cost 3.99% and 4.50% of transaction values, respectively.

As you grow in your cryptocurrency investing skills and become a more sophisticated, high-volume investor, your fee structure will also change to one that’s based on dollar trading volumes for the last 30 days on the ActiveTrader system. 

Liquidity levels of specific digital coins also have an impact on your fees under this trading volume-heavy fee structure. For example, you’ll have to pay a “taker” fee if the coin you want to buy is immediately available. If you’re on the selling side and your transaction has no immediate buyer yet, you’ll have to pay a “maker” fee. And if you want to minimize and maximize your block cryptocurrency transaction buying and selling prices, respectively, you may use ActiveTrader’s “auction” option, too.
