## Chapter 8

 

## Cryptocurrency Mining Explained

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

Ask as many people as you like what they think cryptocurrency mining is. Some will look at you like you've grown an extra head, while many others will say it's just a way of getting more crypto coins. In a way, it is, but it involves so much more. Mining is also about validating transactions on the blockchain and adding them in blocks to the ledger. Perhaps its most important job, though, is to prevent double-spending. 

Think about the physical fiat currency you spend. When you make a purchase, your bank updates your account, showing the payment leaving your account and going into another one. It works the same way with cryptocurrencies. When a crypto purchase is made, the digital ledger is updated, showing the debit from one account and the credit to another. However, digital platforms are constantly being hacked or manipulated. The Bitcoin ledger overcame this challenge by creating a system where transactions could only be updated on the ledger by verified miners. This ensures those miners are responsible for ensuring double-spending cannot take place. 

While the miners keep the network secure, new crypto coins are being generated. These are given to the miners to reward them for their work. Because there is no central authority on a distributed ledger, mining is critical to ensure transactions are validated, hence the incentive for the miners to take part - if they validate the transaction, they win the reward.  

A consensus protocol is in place to ensure that only those verified miners can do the work. This is known as a proof-of-work protocol, and it also keeps the network secured against outside attacks. 

Proof-of-Work

You can think of cryptocurrency mining as akin to mining precious gems or metals. While these miners will uncover diamonds, gold, silver, etc., cryptocurrency miners unearth new coins and bring them into circulation. A miner needs to use a powerful machine to solve complicated mathematical problems - cryptographic hashes to earn the rewards on offer. A hash is a shortened digital signature representing the data in the transaction, generated to keep the data on public networks secure. Miners compete against one another to solve the hash value that each transaction generates - the first one to solve it adds the transaction block to the blockchain and gets the reward. 

The hash function for each transaction block contains a reference to the previous block. This continues right back to the first block, known as the genesis block, creating an unbroken chain. This is why each network node can verify and validate blocks and can verify whether the winning miner correctly solved the hash.

As more miners join the network and more powerful computation resources are used, the equations become more difficult to solve. This increases the competition and adds to the cryptocurrency's scarcity. 

How to Start Mining

To start mining on any cryptocurrency blockchain, you need a powerful computer with the right software designed to solve the cryptographic equations. In the early days of blockchain technology, you could mine simple cryptocurrencies with a home computer containing a simple CPU chip. However, as the difficulty levels have risen over the years, those CPU chips no longer do the job.

Today, you need one of two things:

*   An ASIC (application-specific integrated circuit) miner
*   A specialized GPU

The GPUs must also be connected to a fast, reliable internet connection 24/7. Each cryptocurrency miner must also be part of an online mining pool.

### Different Mining Methods

The mining method you choose will dictate the length of time it takes. In the early days, CPU mining was the chosen method. However, it is incredibly slow and is no longer practical because of the length of time needed to accrue a tiny profit - it can take months, and mining rigs use a significant amount of electricity and cooling. The cost is often way beyond what the profits are.

GPU mining is now a more common method, using a set of GPUs in a single mining rig to maximize the computational power. The rig also requires a motherboard and an effective cooling system.

ASIC mining is also becoming popular. ASIC mining rigs are designed ONLY to mine cryptos, unlike GPU rigs that can be used as ordinary, albeit powerful, computers. Because of this, ASIC miners can produce more crypto units, but they are more expensive - the more difficult mining becomes, the more likely it is that these rigs will disappear.

Cloud mining is slowly taking over, eliminating the rising costs of ASIC and GPU mining. People who do cloud mining can leverage power from dedicated mining facilities and large corporations. As an individual miner, you can find paid and free mining hosts and rent a rig for a set period, making it a hands-free mining method. 

Mining Pools

While you are perfectly at liberty to mine alone, many people choose to join a mining pool. All the miners' resources are combined, providing more computational power to increase the chances of solving the equation and earning the reward. Should a pool succeed, the reward is split among all members, proportionate to the level of resources each contributes. 

Most cryptocurrency mining applications already have a mining pool, but you also have the option of creating a pool by joining other like-minded enthusiasts online. Once you join a pool, you are not tied to staying with it - you can move between pools as much as you want, and many people move because some pools earn higher rewards.

However, many miners feel that the official pools are better - they are more reliable, offer technical support, and are updated regularly by the hosting companies. If you think joining a mining pool might be for you, have a look at CryptoCompare to compare the different ones available based on profitability, reliability, and different crypto coins. 

Is Crypto Mining Worth It?

Whether mining is right for you is a matter of personal choice, and several factors will affect your decision. Regardless of the type of mining you choose - CPU, ASIC, GPU, or Cloud - perhaps the most important things to consider are:

*   The hash rate produced by the mining rig
*   Power consumption
*   Overall costs

Typically, a good cryptocurrency mining rig will cost anywhere between $3000 and $10000+. They also give off a lot of heat and use high amounts of electricity. For example, a typical ASIC miner used to create one Bitcoin every 10 minutes will use roughly 72 terawatts of power - 1 terawatt is sufficient to power 10 billion 100-watt lightbulbs simultaneously, just to give you an idea of how much power that is! As blockchain technology gets better and the mining difficulty becomes harder, that power consumption figure will change. You also need to factor in the costs for keeping the rig cool, especially when using ASIC or GPU rigs. 

Lastly, you must consider the difficulty level surrounding the cryptos you want to mine to decide if there is enough profit in it for you. 

Tax Implications

Tax is also something you need to consider. I'll be covering this in more detail in a later chapter, but for now, you need to know that cryptocurrency miners will typically have to pay tax in two cases:

1.  When they receive a crypto reward for mining, and
2.  When they exchange or sell their reward tokens or coins

In the first case, Notice 2014-2021 has been issued by the IRS to address the tax issues surrounding crypto mining. According to the notice, miners must declare an amount of gross income for each crypto reward they get; that amount must be a fair indication of the coin or token value at the time of receipt. If the mining is considered part of a business, the tokens or coins are treated as self-employment income and are subject to the relevant taxes. Similarly, if the miner works for someone else, their crypto payments are seen as wages and are subject to income tax laws, withholding unemployment taxes, Medicare, and social security.

Is Mining Legal?

Right now, few laws govern cryptos, so, in most countries, it is not clear whether mining is legal or not. Under the FinCEN (Financial Crimes Enforcement Network), miners are classed as "money transmitters, " meaning the laws governing that activity may apply to them. For example, cryptocurrency mining is seen as a business in Israel and comes under corporate income tax laws. Elsewhere, there is a lot of regulatory uncertainty, although some countries, such as the USA and Canada, appear to be okay with it. 

However, other than a few jurisdictions that have made crypto-related activities illegal, few countries stop people from mining. 

Is Cryptocurrency Mining Sustainable?

Most cryptocurrency miners have plenty of curiosity and a desire to learn, two things that are a must for anyone thinking of joining in. New technologies are constantly emerging, and the mining arena is always changing. The miners who get the best rewards are those who study and optimize their methods and strategies to improve continually.

However, some advocates for climate change have voiced concerns about the level of fossil fuels crypto mining burns through. Concerns like these have led some communities, like Ethereum, to move from a Proof-of-Work framework to something more sustainable, such as Proof-of-Stake. 

I haven’t gone into too much detail on mining. For most people considering investing in cryptocurrencies, the easiest route is to buy and sell rather than spend thousands on setting up a rig where the rewards may be little. To be fair, the only people who are really benefiting from mining are those who did it in the early days of Bitcoin when the prices were low. 

The next chapter will dive into some strategies for short-term trading.

## Chapter 9

 

## Strategies for Short-Term Trading

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

Investing in cryptocurrencies is a high-risk endeavor, perhaps more so than traditional trading. Cryptos are digital assets, a new asset class with a short history, and anyone thinking of investing must be aware that their investments can disappear quickly.

![](Images/image00343.jpeg)

As an investor, you should take time to familiarize yourself with the rules and regulations surrounding tax and cryptocurrency profits. If you choose the most popular short-term strategy, day-trading, crypto sales are subject to short-term capital gains tax. 

Before we look at some strategies, here are some of the pros and cons of trading in cryptocurrency:

Pros

*   If you have access to a crypto exchange, you can freely trade in cryptocurrency. Besides verifying who you are and putting money in your account, short-term traders have few barriers to their market access. If you want to own Bitcoin but can't afford the current price, you can buy and sell fractions of it.
*   Crypto markets work 24/7, 365 days a year. There are no limits to buying and selling, and you can trade whenever you want to. 
*   Cryptos and blockchain technology are both decentralized, ensuring anonymity and no intermediary fees, keeping the cost of transactions low. 

Cons

*   The ease with which you can start trading means it is also easy for newbies to lose big money as easily as they can gain big profits.
*   The market being open 24/7 isn't always a good thing. It means there are few trading patterns to follow, especially if you choose day-trading. You may find yourself watching things for much longer than you would in the traditional markets to ensure you execute your trade at the right time. Trends are not so easy to spot, and a quick session can turn into an all-nighter. 
*   Although FinCen and other government watchdogs are gradually increasing anti-money laundering and KYC requirements, the crypto space is largely unregulated. That lack of regulation means that traders and exchanges have no protection from hackers and scammers. If your chosen exchange is subject to an attack, unless they have insurance (highly unlikely), your funds are likely gone for good. 
*   You are also at risk of wash-sales or pump-and-dump schemes. These happen when people pump the price of a cryptocurrency up artificially in a bid to pull in traders. When the traders earn their profits, they dump the crypto, and the price falls fast. 

Let's look at some of the best strategies to get you started in short-term trading:

Choose Your Platform

While it may be tempting to act like a kid in a sweet shop where crypto trading is concerned, you must take your time and choose the right platform to trade on. Remember that if you are based in the USA, you are limited to domestically regulated platforms.

Several factors will determine the platform you choose - asset and exchange liquidity, fees, etc. - you also need to consider volatility because crypt values can spiral up and down without warning. If you are in it for a quick profit, you must be in a position to move fast when needed. 

What doesn't help is that there are so many differences between the crypto exchanges, so here are the most important things you need to consider:

*   How Long the Exchange Has Been Active - the longer it has operated, the more chance it has of being reliable, stable, and credible.
*   The Cryptos Supported - this is one of the most valuable tools to a trader; there is little point in joining an exchange only to find that you have no interest in the supported cryptos or it only offers limited support. 
*   The Fees - these are charged for each trade and are how the exchange earns their money. The fee structures between exchanges will vary - some don't charge any fees for trades of more than $10,000, for example, as an incentive for traders interested in large-scale trades
*   Their Deposit Methods - you need to get money into your exchange account, so the payment methods offered are important. Some will accept wires and bank transfers, but only a few will allow you to fund your account with a credit card, gift card, or PayPal
*   Reviews - look on the crypt communities to find reviews and feedback on exchanges. Most users on these forums will provide honest feedback, be it negative or positive
*   Exchange Liquidity - this covers the ease and speed with which an exchange converts assets with no price effect. The best exchanges have high liquidity, meaning they can buy and sell immediately with no discernible difference between the bid and asking price. 

Choose Your Investments

The exchange is just the first step. Next, you need to decide what you want to invest in. If you opt for day-trading, keep volatility in mind - where a market condition could put buy-and-hold (long-term) investors off, it could be just what a day trader to grab a decent profit. New traders are advised to invest in the biggest coins or tokens, but this doesn't always pan out. Critically, you need to research what's available against how much you want to invest and determine if it's worth the risk. You can only do that through careful research of a specific coin or token, how it has behaved in the past and what it's doing now. 

Consider the following when deciding what cryptocurrency to invest in:

*   Asset Liquidity - this indicates whether an asset can easily be converted without affecting the price. Crypto prices can easily change between a trade being executed and the order being fulfilled by the exchange, and the price difference is known as slippage. A minute or two can be the difference between profit and loss in crypto trading. 
*   Understanding Volatility - we all know the crypto market is volatile, and factors affecting it include research reports, news, and economic data, sometimes causing dramatic impacts. Altcoins tend to be more volatile, especially those with smaller market caps, than the larger, more stable cryptos.
*   Understanding Trading Volume - this measures overall interest in the market, and cryptos with a higher trading volume are usually more liquid. By the same token, high-liquidity cryptos are usually higher priced. However, not all cryptos were created equal, and you should always look hard at trading volume to ascertain if an asset can be sold for a profit and quickly.

Finding the right crypto asset means finding one with volatility and liquidity, especially for day traders. However, you should also consider other factors - how is the crypto minted or mined? Is it surrounded by hype? When should you take your losses and get out if the price doesn't go up within a set time limit?

Choose Your Trading Strategy

There are plenty of techniques for short-term traders, and creating the right strategy can only be done with proper research and a plan. Some exchanges allow you to follow a prominent trader and copy their trades, but that means taking losses as well as gains. 

Here are some of the more popular short-term trading strategies:

### Arbitrage

Arbitrage is when you purchase crypto on an exchange and then sell it straight away on a different platform for a profit. This is best done with crypto pairs, especially where the pair comprises Bitcoin and a less well-known altcoin and the prices are different on other exchanges. Although arbitrage is more complicated than many other financial mechanisms, and price-monitoring software usually automates the process, it does tend to stabilize asset prices between the exchanges. 

### Bot Trading

These are automated software tools traders use to make trades at set times or when pre-set conditions are met for the best profit. Crypto bots were specifically designed to reduce risk, cut losses, and increase gains. They can make the process of studying fees, price movements, and opportunities for gains more streamlined. This is definitely a must if you have some experience with APIs and software programming.

### Long Straddle

Also called the option straddle, buy straddle, or just a straddle, requires a trade to purchase a put and call order together. Both orders must be on the same crypto and share the same striking price and date of expiration.

The crypto market volatility makes this a great short-term trading option. In short, you are betting on the crypto's price changing, and, in day trading specifically, whether the price rises or falls, the trader makes a profit. The only time they don't is when the price stays still - then, they take a loss. 

### Range Trading

You can capitalize on non-trending markets with range trading by studying charts to find resistance and support levels. The technique allows you to identify a specific period to buy your asset at a low price (when it is oversold) and sell it at a higher price (when it is overbought) to make a profit. 

You can use online screeners to determine the right asset for this technique, and it is highly used because its entry and exit points are clearly defined. Because the trader can make smaller profits over a short time, they keep the losses caused by market conditions and bad news to a minimum. 

### Scalping

Scalping has the fastest turnaround of all short-term trading strategies. Traders can take advantage of small movements in price by leveraging large liquidity amounts. The strategy requires you to buy and see altcoins or Bitcoins over a matter of hours and cash out at the end of the day on small percentage gains. Alternatively, you can use a bot to monitor technical indicators or signals to carry our frequent, high-intensity trades. This is one of the easiest ways to use automated bots and is low risk. While the daily gains may be small, they can add up to quite a big one over time. 

### Technical Analysis

Technical analysis is an important part of any trading strategy. It involves studying cryptocurrency's financial data, such as the volume data points and historical prices. In short, it gives you the science to find the right trading opportunities and earn the maximum profits. 

Technical analysts can use their strategy in any security or market where historical trading data is present. In crypto trading, it helps you use past performance to get an idea of the future. 

However, the use of technical analysis in cryptocurrency trading has come under some debate. Some say it isn't compatible with cryptocurrency's decentralized, unregulated nature. Others say that some indicators are worth using, such as RSI (relative strength index). This indicator can tell us whether a security or asset has been oversold or underbought, helping traders who use the range trading strategy. 

Another indicator worth using, and one that should be used with RSI, is MFI (Money Flow Index) - together, they can tell you if Bitcoin is heading towards a bearish or bullish divergence. 

Technical analysis should never be used alone - always use it with other strategies to get the best results.

## Chapter 10

 

## Strategies for  
Long-Term Investing

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

While trading can be short or medium term, investing tends to be mid to long-term. The idea behind it is to place your money into one or more assets and leave it to earn interest or sell it when the price is high. Long-term investors can plan for years ahead and grow their savings in this way, but the cryptocurrency market does not make long-term investing very easy.

![](Images/image00344.jpeg)

With cryptos, long-term investing means investing for at least a year, and it requires far more in-depth knowledge than short-term trading. Because your investments should be held for a minimum of a year, getting a high return involves a certain amount of risk. 

Those risks are much higher in terms of cryptocurrency, partly because of an unfamiliar and fairly new market. However, as time passes, it becomes better-known, and some of the larger financial institutions are starting to take notice of it. Long-term crypto investment is an excellent opportunity for traditional traders who want some portfolio diversification. 

Long-Term Investing Explained

Long-term investment strategies typically seek profit by holding assets for a minimum of a year but usually more than three. Let's say Company A has a large stake in company B but doesn't have a majority of the voting shares. The stake purchase cost should go on the balance sheet, and the short-term investments should go on the profit/loss sheet. When long-term investments are sold, they would not normally have any relation to the profit/loss but, instead, classed as another type of comprehensive income. 

Here's a better example;

We have a pair of sisters - Belinda and Jane. Each of them has religiously saved $100 per month towards their long-term investments. After 20 years, they each have $24,000 to invest with an 8% annual return. Belinda began her investments when she was 36 and Jane when she was 446. By the time Belinda reaches 56, she will have $131, 613 and Jane will have $59,295.

In this example, the return is double the original investment because Belinda started saving ten years before Jane. This shows how long-term investments can benefit you far more than short-term ones because of the interest accruing every year. 

When you hold a long-term investment in the crypto market, it is called a HODL. This is an incorrectly spelled version of the word HOLD and became popular after the misspelling was posted on Bitcointalk in 2013.

### Why Would You Invest Long-Term in a Volatile Market?

Long-term investments are the most reliable method of maximizing a stable asset's returns. Many traders who don't know how to do proper technical analysis or have little to no knowledge of market behavior tend to rely on long-term investments. It does have a distinct advantage if not requiring daily, weekly, or monthly monitoring - it can simply be left until the time is right. 

Long-term investing is considered a good way of diversifying a portfolio using emerging markets, like cryptocurrencies, while having short-term investments in stocks and other traditional markets. 

Suitable Crypto Types for Long-Term Investment

You can invest in the crypto market in two ways:

*   HODL
*   Active Trading

When investors choose the first way, they typically purchase cryptos such as Bitcoin and hold them for a long period. They forget about the short-term market fluctuations and wait until the price is high enough to maximize profit. Not all cryptos are suitable for this type of investment, but some that got through the crypto crash in 2018 relatively unscathed are considered the best, given their apparent stability and acceptance by investors. 

Here are five that survived and are considered excellent choices for long-term investment:

*   Bitcoin
*   Ethereum
*   Litecoin
*   Monero
*   XRP (formerly Ripple)

Other cryptos worth long-term investments are those that yield dividends. When you invest for the long term, you don't just have to choose cryptos that will hopefully rise in price; you can also choose those that pay dividends and hopefully rise as well. Some of the best are:

*   Decred (DCR)
*   Komodo (KMD)
*   Navcoin (NAV)
*   Neblio (NEL)
*   Neo (NEO)
*   PIVX (PIVX)
*   Reddcoin (RDD)
*   VeChain (VET)

However, you must be aware that you need a systematic approach to investing in the crypto market, and you must have the right strategy to be successful.

Investment Strategy

No matter what we want in life, having a plan is critical to ensure success. Long-term investments are no exception to this. Your strategies must include an investment plan covering the following points:

*   Your investment objectives
*   How long you plan to hold your investments
*   Tax implications
*   Entry/exit plan

Your strategy must be well-researched and effective, with a history showing it provides returns. With crypto investing, though, you need a little bit more, including some effective, exclusive strategies.

One of the most effective is HODL, where investors only hold the asset. Another is called dollar-cost-averaging, where investors allocate the capital amount at regular intervals. This one is ideal for the smaller investors who struggle to get the capital they need in one hit. 

Success is entirely dependent on how you execute your trades and manage your risks. The maximum output requires the investors to provide a time for when they intend to pull the investment and their risk assessment. In short, a long-term investor must know the length of time they want the investment held and their own personal risk levels. 

How Much Does Long-Term Crypto Investment Cost?

Long-term investments always work out cheaper than short-term trading, but there are some high costs to consider before you dive into the crypto market. Here are some of the costs you need to consider in your plan:

*   Expense Ratio - this indicates the operating expenses, such as admin costs. To keep costs down, look for cryptos with low ratios.
*   Financial Advisory Fees - this is how much you pay to the professionals involved in the trade. Cryptocurrency advisory companies provide rankings or guidance that can help you find the right opportunity for investing long-term.
*   Compound Interest - if you use credit cards or loans to raise your capital, you must factor compound interest into your plan. If you stand to make less from the asset than the interest costs, it's not worth investing in it. 

Are Market Dips Good for Long-Term Investment?

When you "buy the dip," you purchase your crypto when the price has gone down, assuming it will rise again in the future. This requires you to watch the market carefully for a long period to see when the crypto drops to a significantly low price all of a sudden - this is a short-term decline.

Anyone interested in crypto knows of the 2018 crash when Bitcoin plummeted to $5933 from $19675. If you were to purchase at the lower price, which many did, it was considered a buy-dip. However, the nature of the financial markets means they dive about all over the place, giving us swing lows and swing highs. Finding the dip is often not easy, so the best method is to look for a bearish pressure as these often bring about several lows.

You can also consider analysts' reports to understand what the experts think of the price movement. Those who have been in the business a long time and have expert technical analysis knowledge can better interpret these reports. Lastly, consider using an exchange with a low cost and price reliability as one of the best resources to buy dips. 

Your success is dependent on whether the crypto in question is likely to rise again in the future. Again, Bitcoin is an excellent example. During the pandemic, its price crashed, and those who opted to purchase at that time are now rolling in potentially large profits. 

Long-term investment can only be successful and effective if you pick the right crypto to invest in. While it takes knowledge and skill to know what you are doing, there is also a percentage of good luck involved, given market behavior is unpredictable and cryptos are volatile. 

Perhaps one of the best strategies is a mixture of long-term investments and short-term trading, ensuring you benefit all around. You should also have good technical analysis knowledge to ensure you understand what the market is doing.

## Chapter 11

 

## Limiting Losses  
While Making Gains

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

We often hear about cryptocurrency in the news, and many will call it a ruthless market. However, much of this reputation has likely come from investors who took the leap in 2017, when crypto hype was at its highest. Driven by FOMO (fear of missing out) and no small amount of greed, investors threw themselves into the market willy-nilly. Most had no understanding of the cryptocurrency market's fundamentals and set about buying everything they could.

Unsurprisingly, a bubble appeared around cryptocurrencies, and Bitcoin reached a historical high of $20,000 by the time 2017 ended. However, that wasn't to last very long, and the bubble burst in 2018, leading to a lengthy bear market, and all those last-minute investors, fueled by greed, paid for it in spades. 

Things have changed somewhat, and the crypto market is looking pretty bright at the moment. However, while it's easy to say dive in and invest your money, you need to make sure you aren't going to end up with egg on your face, like those earlier investors. To help you, I will give you some tips on maximizing your gains and minimizing your losses. 

Maximize Your Gains

1.  Only Invest What You Can Afford to Lose

This is the number one rule in any type of investing, be it cryptos, stocks, or any other financial market. Why? There are two reasons for this, especially when investing in cryptos:

*   The cryptocurrency market is one of the most volatile in the world.
*   The cryptocurrency market works 24/7, meaning trading never stops.

One of the first traps many new investors fall into is monitoring prices constantly. All that does is tire you out, mentally and physically, causing stress and a much higher chance of making the wrong move. Worn-out investors are also more sensitive to that FOMO feeling. 

To not be exposed to that feeling and make poor trading decisions, you should only ever invest as much you can comfortably afford to lose without risking your life and finances. The best way to do that is to create a budget every month. List down all your income and outgoings and how much you have spare each month. Then, decide on a figure to invest in the crypto market and stick to it rigidly. 

2.  Keep Bitcoin as Your Priority Investment

Bitcoin is constantly in the news, mostly because someone is criticizing it, usually someone from the finance world, regulators, or economists. However, some of those attacks also come from the crypto world. That's because every other cryptocurrency competes with Bitcoin, regardless of how much they say they aren't. Ethereum, Bitcoin Cash, Ripple, EOS, or any of the many others are all after one thing - toppling Bitcoin from its place at the top. 

Never forget this and never forget that Bitcoin still dominates the market, with a 65% market share. Bitcoin is always the first crypto that people think of and is the most decentralized of all cryptocurrencies. This all adds up to Bitcoin being the best investment since it has huge potential for capital appreciation over the long term and offers deep security for any money you invest in it. As such, it should be your investment priority. If you can't afford to buy entire Bitcoins, some exchanges will allow you to purchase and trade in bits of Bitcoins. 

3.  Make Sure Your Crypto Portfolio Is Diversified

While you should make Bitcoin your priority, you should also diversify your portfolio by investing in other cryptocurrencies, especially promising ones. Try for a distribution ratio of 70% Bitcoin and 30% Altcoins. From there, you can do one of two things - increase your Bitcoin share as you see fit, or reduce it and invest in other promising Altcoins. This choice can only be made by assessing your current risk aversion and working out what you can afford to invest. If you think you can comfortably take more risks, reduce Bitcoin to 60% and up your Altcoin investments to 40% of your portfolio, so long as you only choose those with a good chance of increasing in the future. 

You must remember one important thing - rebalance your Altcoins regularly and, on occasion, your Bitcoin percentage. In this way, you can adapt to economic news and events that impact the market and make better profits. 

4.  Listen to Warren Buffet on Fear and Greed

I don't expect you thought you would see Warren Buffet's name in a chapter about maximizing crypto profit. It's no secret that the financial world's biggest investors don’t think much of cryptocurrencies. After all, he once said that "Bitcoin is rat poison squared."

How reassuring is it that a man with an $85 billion+ fortune can sometimes be wrong about money!

However, that same man also said something incredibly smart, something all investors should take note of - "Be fearful when others are greedy and greedy when others are fearful."

Buffet addressed this warning to people investing in traditional markets, but it applies to the cryptocurrency market just as well. No matter what market you invest in, human psychology is the same. 

This means that when most other investors are in FOMO mode and pushing you to get in there and buy some, be very careful. You risk huge disappointment and some not-so-small losses if you fall for it.

Conversely, when many investors are panicking, perhaps because of a news event, and are selling their holdings, be prepared to step out of your comfort zone and put greed to work. This is the time when you can make some excellent investments that are highly likely to pay off handsomely in the future. 

If in doubt, think back to 2018, when investors chose to purchase Bitcoin at the height of the bubble burst when investors were selling to minimize their losses. Those who held their investments, known as HODLERS, are now raking in huge profits. When the market reached its bottom in December 2018, panicking investors were selling for a little over $3,000 per Bitcoin. Those same Bitcoin are now worth more than ten times that amount. 

5.  Research - And Plenty of It

Because Bitcoin and cryptocurrency markets, in general, are so volatile, you must trust in your own instincts when you start investing and trading. To make sure you are in the right position to make the best trades most of the time, you need to be informed, and there is one basic rule for all investors, especially in cryptocurrency - DYOR - Do Your Own Research.

So, first, you must train yourself in how to do technical analysis. This way, you can analyze the price forecasts for the specific assets you want to invest in, rather than going by someone else's analyses.

You also need to take the time to learn about the assets you want to invest in. Read their white papers, trawl the internet and look on social media, particularly Reddit, as they are a mine of useful cryptocurrency information. 

Because you will make your investment decisions based on your research and no one else's, you should not regret any investment you make. From there, you can move easily to the next trade, not panicking about the previous one's outcome. 

Minimizing Your Losses

Over the years since Bitcoin first came into being, the crypto markets have been up and down like yo-yos, even within the bull and bear trends. While we can honestly say that all dips in the crypto market have thus far been followed by a rise, sometimes a significant one, these declines can cause no end of stress, and most investors, especially newbies, find them hard to navigate. 

As these are the time when you are likely to take the most losses, especially if you are not careful, the following tips will help you hang on to your value and keep your losses to a minimum

1.  Don't Fall For FUD and FOMO

While it is imperative to stay updated with the latest crypto trends and news, you can have too much information. This is truer when the market is in decline, when it's easy to lose your nerve, let your instincts over-run you, and make bad decisions.

FUD (fear, uncertainty, and doubt) and FOMO (fear of missing out) are commonly used terms in crypto investing, and both can strongly influence us to buy and sell at the wrong times. 

FUD typically surrounds negative sentiment, usually caused by adverse news, rumors, or a well-known figurehead expressing some concern over an asset or market. This can cause some traders to sell off their investments because they expect the price to continue falling. This negatively affects the price. On the other hand, FOMO is more about traders who get carried away after seeing positive news. They tend to ignore basic signals, in too much of a rush to jump on the bandwagon and shoot for a moon that may be nothing more than a mirage.

The future is not predictable, especially in the cryptocurrency markets, and no one person can give you better advice than you researching for yourself and making your own decisions. Please keep one thing in mind - some publishers and influencers gain from causing FOMO or FUD to send the markets in the direction they want. When you hear or read something about a crypto you are interested in or the markets in general, try to confirm it with several sources, not just one. 

2.  Have Clear Goals, a Diversified Portfolio, and Trade Only What You Can Afford 

You can have as much confidence as you like in a crypto asset, but if you invest more than you can afford, you are in for a world of hurt. Nobody wants to get caught up in a rollercoaster of emotions, watching their portfolio prices drop while waiting for them to rise again.  

Also, any good investor will also have several assets in their portfolio, some short-term and some long-term, and usually a mixture of cryptocurrencies and stocks. 

Because the markets are so volatile and working 24/7, 365 days a year, investors should ensure they have a trading strategy in place before they put any money down, including stating your entry and exit points.

To be fair, you could see every piece of information about crypto, but it only takes one thing - a tweet, a hack, etc. - to send the prices into freefall. This is why planning ahead is critical so you can mitigate your losses in the event of a crash.

In the previous chapter, we looked at some strategies you could follow, and dollar-cost averaging is a good way to help you trade without using your emotions and without the need to constantly stare at charts. Dollar-cost averaging is all about purchasing and selling in small amounts regularly. 

Don't forget - when you hold volatile assets, you can easily find yourself getting carried away. Any type of trading is high-risk, particularly when the market is in a bear period. Investors should set goals to create a balance between potential gains and potential losses. 

3.  Long-Term Investing and HODLing

There's a saying that goes along the lines of "it isn't a loss until you sell." While this isn't 100% true, there is something to it. If your assets have dropped in value since you purchased them, it's called unrealized losses. If you sell them for less than you bought them, they become realized losses.

Over the years, Bitcoin has been in a near-constant uptrend. Even when the prices have temporarily fallen or a bear market persists, historically, we can see that prices do rise eventually - in the case of cryptocurrency, that rise is normally driven by scarcity and other factors. Scarcity is the one factor that leads many people to believe cryptocurrency prices will continue rising. Allowing for your investment timeframe being long-term, you can see negative movements as temporary and can wait them out. 

Holding assets for a longer period, known as HODLING, is a long-proven strategy, and Bitcoin has definitely shown itself to be the most successful asset in the last 10 years.

Don't forget that in some countries, like the USA, holding for long periods can also help you when it comes to taxes, i.e., holding for at least a year can benefit you more than selling early. 

4.  Be Prepared to Take Your Profits or Ride the Dip Out

Perhaps the safest way of avoiding volatility and staying safe during a dip is to convert volatile holdings for stable ones. This way, you can lock your balance in and keep your risks to a minimum. It also reduces the need to be active in your portfolio management and keeps your stress levels down when the market is in a bull period. 

USDC and other stablecoins try to keep their value fixed, and investing in some of these stable assets can keep your exposure to price changes to a minimum when the markets are declining. However, when you sell everything at the same time, it is known as capitulation, and it can lead to crypto investors losing big time if the market suddenly shoots up again. This is why you need a plan detailing roughly what profit and loss levels you can be comfortable with BEFORE you are under pressure and need to make decisions. 

Lots of investors opt to use stable assets in their portfolio, moving in and out of them in a strategy revolving around market withdrawal and a buy-back strategy. In this way, provided they get their timing right, their portfolios gradually increase. However, this is not easy and definitely not for new investors; even experienced investors get their timing wrong. 

5.  Spot the Opportunities

Even if the markets are in freefall, you can always find opportunities - you just need to know where to look. Where some might see doom and gloom, keen-eyed investors will see a ray of light where they can get the right asset at the right price and wait for the profits to come rolling in. 

One of the most popular ways to do this is to buy the dip. This gets you into the market or helps you rase your current position. Even in downtrends, the market still fluctuates, and prices still go up at times. Provided you have technical analysis skills, you can benefit from using your knowledge to predict those movements, buying the lows and selling the highs.

Another good strategy during market dips is to short sell, which means betting that the price will fall on a specific asset. DeFi yield farming and staking can also level your returns out, ensuring your crypto balance continues to grow when others around you are falling like dominos. 

And again, the fallback is dollar-cost averaging, especially where you think an asset is definitely going to rise because this strategy works whether the markets rise or fall. In fact, with dollar-cost averaging, you stand to gain more when the market is down. 

Don't forget that these activities are not for those of a nervous disposition and, done wrong, could lose you a lot of money. They could also lead to you spending more time than you need to watching price charts and getting further stressed out.

Next, we will look at something called IDDA - the Invest Diva Diamond Analysis - a very important strategy for any investor.

## Chapter 12

 

## The IDDA Strategy

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

IDDA stands for Invest Diva Diamond Analysis. Before you go ahead and execute your newly drawn-up strategy, you need to use some different methods to analyze the markets, and many traders choose just one or two. However, this is a dangerous way to go; IDDA suggest using five points to analyze the markets, and we're going to discuss those now:

![](Images/image00345.jpeg)

Point One - Fundamental analysis

This is when you use every news story, economic event, gossip, and the financial situation of the crypto you want to invest in and consider potential upcoming events that could affect the market. 

Fundamental analysts use all this data to determine whether to invest in an asset by analyzing the political, social, and economic forces affecting a crypto's supply and demand.

However, although this is all valuable data, it can be overwhelming, but this is what you should be looking at:

*   Company and industry information from sources like Yahoo Finance, Motley Fool, NASDAQ, etc.
*   Economic and current events from sources like WSJ and Barron's financial journals, Washington Post, New York Times, Bloomberg, CNBC, etc.

However, we all know that the internet is full of scams and avoiding false news is imperative. Two of the most common scams are:

*   Those that promise a get-rich-quick scheme (no such thing in most investing, especially not crypto investing)
*   Pump-and dumps - promoters purchase specific cryptos, pump the price up by creating a lot of hype and then dump it at a high price, thus knocking the price back down again. 

You can find plenty of information on avoiding these scams at [www.sec.gov](http://www.sec.gov).

Point Two - Technical Analysis

This requires you to look at the trading charts and analyze the price action history and revolves around attempting to predict what a particular crypto's future might hold. The main difference between this and fundamental analysis can be seen in a shopping example. A fundamental analyst looking for the right shop to buy a wedding present would ask their friends what they think of the products sold in several different shops. Then you would visit each store, look at each product and decide what to buy. A technical analyst would sit and watch each store. They would analyze what each shopper has in their bag, what their facial expressions are before and after they shop, and so on, and they would base their decision on certain patterns. 

You know the old saying, "history repeats itself" - that is technical analysis in a nutshell. In short, you look for past patterns and use them to create a strategy based on the crypto repeating those price movements. The best way to do this is to look at each cryptocurrency's charts as you can see price movements and trends quite easily. 

Point Three - Market Sentiment Analysis 

This revolves around traders' attitudes and emotions regarding a particular crypto and is the study of the bulls and the bears. When traders expect a price to go up, the market sentiment is bullish. Conversely, the sentiment is bearish when they expect the price to go down. 

All traders have their own intuition or feelings, so purely studying prices won't tell you very much. Their opinions are expressed in whether they take a bearish or bullish position in the market, thus adding to the overall sentiment. 

Point Four - Capital Analysis

This is quite possibly the most important of the five points and is the one most ignored by retail traders. Capital analysis requires you to research your risks, capital, and potential for reward. This is where calculating risk tolerance comes in, one of the most important steps to do before you decide on a trading strategy. 

You must calculate your available capital, leverage that makes the most sense, and the spread with your chosen broker; otherwise, you will fall prey to unpredictable losses. This kind of this is also known as risk management. 

Your trading strategy will depend entirely on how much money is in your account and your capital. When you enter and exit a trade, your broker requires a fee known as the spread - this is the difference between your bid and the ask price. It is not recommended to speculate or trade right before a risk event - if you've done your fundamental analysis, you'll know what's what. 

Point Five - Overall Analysis

This is where you get an overall analysis of the previous four points. You need to know a couple of things:

*   First, are 90% or more of the previous points telling you a trade will be successful?
*   Are you comfortable executing the trade, or does your gut tell you to hold off?

We've all heard the term "women's intuition," and there is much research indicating that women can tell what someone is feeling through a kind of psychic ability. However, where trading is concerned, it doesn't mean you have to bring your wife/mother/sister in to help you because men use their gut feelings too - if you trust yours, it will rarely put you wrong. 

Instinct or intuition can do two things - it can be your starting point for analysis and a final indicator of whether you should go ahead. The one piece of advice I really can give you here is this - if you are not sure, if something is telling you not to do the trade, don't do it. 

Never execute trades based purely on intuition, and always make sure you don't confuse it with greed and/or fear. Always use all five points to ensure the trade is right for you. 

The next chapter will discuss the tax system and crypto investments.

## Chapter 13

 

## A Word about Taxes

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

The last couple of years have been pretty wild for the cryptocurrency market. Over 10% of Americans traded, and more than a few wonder what the score is regarding taxes. Many new crypto investors think that they are exempt from tax, but this isn't the case, unfortunately. Anyone who trades in crypto must report conversions, sales, payments, and their income from crypto to the state tax authorities and IRS where applicable. What makes it harder is that each type of transaction is subject to different tax rules. This chapter will cover those rules for you, so you are in no doubt what you need to report. Before we move on, though, I must tell you that you should speak to a tax professional experienced in crypto taxes to ensure you get things right. 

![](Images/image00346.jpeg)

Do I Owe Taxes on My Crypto?

Cryptocurrency is a digital asset and is considered as such in the USA. Because of this, it is treated in the same way the IRS treats bonds, stocks, and capital assets. This means that any money gained from your crypto is taxed at all different rates, dependent on the method used to obtain the crypto and the length of time you held it. 

So, how do you know if you owe any taxes? The most important thing is to look at how your crypto was used in the previous year, in this case, 2021. A crypto transaction where tax is due is called a "taxable event," while those where tax is not due are called "non-taxable events. Let's break down what each involves:

### Non-Taxable Events:

*   Purchasing and Holding Crypto - you do not need to declare tax on cryptocurrency when you just purchase and hold it. That comes later when the crypto is sold and you "realize" the gains.
*   Donations to Non-Profit or Tax-Exempt Charities - provided the charity is a registered 501(c)(3) organization, you can donate crypto without incurring taxes and may even be able to claim charitable deductions.
*   Receiving Crypto as a Gift - if someone gifts you some crypto, you will not need to pay tax unless you sell it, stake it, or partake in any other taxable activity.
*   Giving Crypto as a Gift - you can gift each recipient $15,000 or less per year without needing to pay tax - that amount is higher for a spouse. If you go over $15,000, a gift tax return must be filed. However, this doesn't usually generate a tax liability. Whenever you send crypto to another person, and it is not classed as paying for a service or goods, it may be considered a gift, even if it was intended that way.
*   Transferring Between Your Own Wallets/Accounts \- this is not considered taxable. In preparation for an eventual trade or sale, it is possible to transfer the original cost basis and the date you acquired the asset so you can track your tax impact.

### Taxable Events - Capital Gains

*   Selling Cryptocurrency for Cash - if you sell your crypto and receive U.S. dollars in exchange, you are liable for tax on any amount above what you paid for the asset. For example, if you purchased crypto for $1000 and sell it for $1500, you must pay tax on the additional $500. However, if you sell for less than you paid, you may be able to show it as a loss.
*   Converting Crypto to Another Crypto - for example, you use Bitcoin to purchase Ether. Technically, your Bitcoin must be sold before purchasing Ether, and the IRS treats this as a taxable sale. If you sell for more than you originally paid, you must pay taxes on the price difference.
*   Using Crypto to Buy Goods and Services - any transaction where crypto is used to buy a service or goods, tax is due. According to the IRS, there is little difference between spending and selling crypto - in these transactions, the crypto is sold before it can be exchanged for services or goods, making it a taxable transaction.

### Taxable Event - Income

*   Receiving Payment in Crypto - if you receive your salary in crypto, you are liable for income tax as it is classed as compensation, and you are taxed per your tax bracket. 
*   Receiving Crypto as Payment for Services or Goods - this is taxable as it is treated the same way as a sale. It is classed as income, and you must report it as such. 
*   Mining Cryptocurrency - if you are a crypto miner and earn rewards/transaction frees, you may have to pay taxes. This is based on a fair market value of the coins when you receive them. If you are mining crypto as a business concern, it must be registered as self-employment income. 
*   Receiving Staking Rewards - in the same way as earnings from mining, these earnings are taxable, based on a fair market value on the day you receive the reward.
*   Other Income - if you hold cryptos and earn a return from them, it is considered income and is taxable as such. Although this may be interest paid on your holdings, the IRS doesn't treat it in the same way as interest earned on your bank account.
*   Receiving an Airdrop - crypto airdrops are when you receive them as part of a giveaway or marketing campaign. This is classed as taxable income and must be reported as such.
*   Receiving Other Rewards or Incentives - this could be from incentives, friend referrals to certain exchanges, and so on - anything like this must be reported as taxable income. 

How Much Do I Owe?

So, you've probably determined that you will owe taxes on some of your crypto activities. What do you do now? Well, you could estimate your taxes by working out your income, losses, and gains. 

### Calculating Your Income

As a U.S. taxpayer, you are used to seeing your state and federal income tax deducted straight from your pay. Any crypto assets earned as income must be declared as income and taxes paid. However, these are not treated the same way as your salaried income, i.e., the taxes won't be automatically deducted, and you must report them on your yearly income tax form. The taxes owed on this income is determined based on your tax bracket. However, be aware of one thing - if you have earned a high level of additional income from your crypto assets, you may be moved up a tax bracket or so and will have to pay higher taxes on your earnings. This will include your fiat money earnings, such as salary. 

### Calculating Your Capital Gains and Losses

Before calculating this, you must know the amount of crypto you had at the start of the tax year. This is known as your cost basis. When you purchase crypto, this is based on the amount you paid for the asset. However, if your crypto came from staking or mining, a fair market value is used to work out the cost basis based on when you receive the crypto. In terms of crypto you received as a gift, this depends on the cost basis of the person gifting it to you and a fair market value on the day you received it. 

If you sell your cryptocurrency, the cost basis is deducted from the sale price to determine if you have made a capital gain (if it is more than your cost basis) or a capital loss (when it is less).

### Short-Term vs. Long-Term Capital Gains

These taxes are applied at the state level, where applicable, and federal level and may be short or long-term. The length of time you have held your assets will determine how much tax you must pay. If you have held them for at least 12 months, the tax rate is typically lower than if you held them for less. 

*   Long-Term Gains - these are typically taxed at a lower capital gains tax rate. At the federal level, these rates are 0%, 15%, and 20% and are based on your total income. Those taxpayers in the higher income tax brackets may also have to pay a Net Investment Income Tax on income and gains, currently 3.8%.
*   Short-Term Gains - these are typically taxed at your current income tax rate, usually a higher rate, which makes it worthwhile holding onto your assets for at least a year before selling them.

Don't forget; when you sell your crypto for cash, convert it into a different crypto asset, or purchase goods or services with it, it results in a taxable event because you have "realized" the losses or gains. If you continue to hold the original crypto, your gains or losses are "unrealized."

### Understanding Capital Losses

If you sell a crypto asset for less than you originally bought it for, you have "realized" a capital loss. However, these aren't always bad news and can sometimes work to your advantage. Losses can be used to offset your capital gains, even when they come from stocks or other non-crypto holdings. This offset can be done on a dollar-for-dollar basis and can reduce your tax liability for the year. 

If your losses are far higher than your gains or you don't "realize" any gains at all, there is a limit to how much you can declare as losses to offset your liabilities. That limit is $3,000 per year - if your losses are higher, anything over $3,000 is carried over to the following year, and this will continue until the total loss amount has been applied. 

After all of this, if you still want to invest in cryptocurrency, read the last two chapters to get some tips on what to do and what challenges you will likely face, not to mention the opportunities up for grabs.

## Chapter 14

 

## Things to Consider  
before Starting

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

Up to this point, you've learned what cryptocurrency is and how it works. We've discussed different types of cryptos, looked at how the blockchain works, and even looked at some long and short-term strategies you can use. In the last chapter, we looked at taxes, so you should now know if you are ready to go ahead with investing in crypto. Before you take that final step, I'm giving you a list of things you should consider before you go all-in.

1.  Don't Get Over-Excited

Starting a new adventure is exciting, no matter what it is, and in some cases, getting a head start can put you ahead of everyone else. However, cryptocurrency investing is no different from any other type of investment. In fact, perhaps more so, it requires strict risk management, discipline, and more than a little patience. Crypto investing should never be seen as a scheme to get rich quick - sure, you've sat with your jaw dropped, watching Bitcoin rise to massive prices in the past, but whether that happens again is a different matter. The crypt bubble has burst, and the chances of those heady heights being reached again are low. Investors know more than they did back in 2017, and their investment decisions are taken with a clearer head and more calculation - so should yours be.

2.  Know Your Risk Tolerance

Before you dive in, ask yourself these questions:

*   Is cryptocurrency investing the right move for you?
*   How much money should you invest?
*   How much can you afford to lose?
*   Can you stomach the highly volatile nature of the crypto market?
*   Are you patient enough to wait out the dips?

The first step in any investment is knowing your risk tolerance, and it revolves around your ability to handle risk and your willingness to take that risk. Risk tolerance is all about knowing what level of risk you are comfortable with. Given that the crypto market is volatile, not least because of the level of speculation involved, risk tolerance is one of the most important factors in determining your strategies. Let's face it, as we've seen, crypto values can be halved overnight, you could lose your private key, hackers can steal deposits from exchanges, and so on. 

However, your tolerance should not be a set percentage of the total amount you invest in the market; instead, it must be a percentage of the total amount of money invested in your portfolio. 

In short, it is how much you can afford to lose without it affecting your day-to-day life and finances. 

3.  Keep Your Crypto Wallet Protected

When you begin investing in cryptocurrency, you should set up a crypto wallet to secure your purchases. There are many types available, and some exchanges even provide their own. However, these are not the most secure - there have been instances of hacking on these exchanges where significant amounts of crypto have been lost. We talked in an earlier chapter about the different types, so make sure you do your homework and choose a secure wallet to keep your crypto secure. 

4.  Choose the Best Broker or Exchange

Brokers and exchanges are popular places to purchase and trade crypto, but they vary wildly. Some offer only a few cryptos, while others offer a much wider range. Some charge low or no transaction fees; others are much higher. Some are more secure than others, some allow you to top up your account using fiat currency, and others will only allow you to use digital money. We discussed brokers and exchanges earlier, so, as with the wallets, do your homework and make sure you get one that fits your needs and circumstances. Sometimes, you may be better having more than one, or exchange and a broker, again depending on your requirements.

5.  Decide Whether You Want Long or Short-Term Investing

This will come down to several factors:

*   Your risk tolerance
*   Financial/investment goals
*   Financial situation now
*   How much time do you have?

For example, if you work a full-time job, you won't want to spend the rest of your time worrying about managing a portfolio of short-term investments. You won't have time for a start, and if you spend all your downtime focusing on that, you won't be fit for your main paying job. Long-term investing requires much less attention and is often less risky too. Most people opt for a balance between the two - a couple of short-term investments and several longer-term ones. And don't forget that long-term investments are also subject to lower rates of tax.

6.  Don't Dive in at the Deep End

In other words, start small. If you are just dipping your toes in and haven't yet drawn up a financial plan, there is little point in throwing a whole heap of money at the market - that's a surefire way to lose everything. Set a limit - perhaps a couple of hundred dollars - and stick to it. Start with just one crypto, too, rather than spreading your money too thin. Also, if this is your first time, don't use all your funds just for crypto; diversify and have a few stock, foreign exchange, or exchange-traded funds too. Eventually, you will get your portfolio just right and know exactly what you need to invest in and how much.

7.  Know Your Cause and Follow It

Almost every cryptocurrency available today is based on a blockchain application that solves a particular world or social problem. Whether it is providing banking to those who can't get it or are underbanked or helping prevent voter fraud, something will suit what you want. When you invest your money in specific crypto with an appealing cause, you are helping it achieve its goals. This ensures that crypto investing has some meaning to you, and it also helps you choose the cryptos you want to invest in from the many available. 

8.  Consider Mining

Many cryptocurrencies rely on mining to go into circulation. Take Bitcoin, for example; without mining, there wouldn't be a Bitcoin currency or market. Mining requires network nodes to solve complex problems using electricity and powerful computers. If you successfully solve a problem, you earn a crypto reward. However, many people find it too expensive because of the investment required to set up a mining rig and the electricity required to run it. A lot will depend on the crypto's value. If your setup costs and electricity bill are less than what you could make on the crypto, it may be a consideration. Alternatively, you could join a mining pool. 

9.  Consider Other Assets before Cryptos

If you are new to investing, not just in cryptocurrency in anything, you will likely find things somewhat overwhelming. It isn't always easy to find the right investment strategy while educating yourself about the crypto sector, so starting with foreign exchange or stocks might be best. Once you are comfortable with the ins and outs of investing, then you could consider moving into cryptocurrencies and growing your portfolio. Forex trading is often the easiest way to start - trading one currency for another, i.e., US dollar for Euro or Japanese Yen. 

10.  Make Sure You Have a Support Group

Most people sit at their computers or phones and trade by themselves, but that's quite lonely, especially when you don't know anyone else who is doing it. You don't have anyone to talk to, and things can get frustrating, especially when everything seems to be against you. Sometimes, you need someone else to tell you when to get out of the market. 

Technical analysis and thorough research can help you identify entry and exit points, but it can all be a bit too much when you are new to investing. Getting together with others in the same boat as you can be very helpful, and there are plenty of forums like Reddit and Telegram channels where you can connect with other investors and help each other out. You will also find a lot of expert traders on these channels, and they can often point you in the right direction. These forums are also useful for helping you find the right assets to invest in, finding out about exchanges, and others may even ask questions you never even considered—all useful stuff. In fact, I would recommend you join these forums before you start investing, just to get an idea of what help is available and get advice from those in the know.

## Chapter 15

 

## Challenges and Opportunities

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

Our final chapter introduces you to some of the challenges and opportunities cryptocurrency investing can put your way. There is a good reason why I have put these together, and that is simply because an obstacle doesn't always have to be a bad thing - treat it right, and it could become an opportunity that leads to profit. 

![](Images/image00347.jpeg)

 

1.  New Cryptocurrencies

The world's first cryptocurrency, Bitcoin, is now nearly 24 years old, but while it is still the most popular one, plenty of alternatives are available. Good or bad, new cryptos are appearing with more and more regularity these days, and that isn't going to change anytime soon. Granted, many of them will fade away to nothing and never be seen again, but a handful are likely to go somewhere, and a few may even get somewhere near Bitcoin and Ethereum. There could even be one right now, waiting to be developed, that could take the world by surprise and displace Bitcoin from the number one spot. 

You can see this as a challenge and an opportunity. Cryptocurrency investing requires you to keep an open mind and draw up your vision for the future. You need to be able to see through the new cryptos and choose those with promising potential, and you can't do that if you are stuck on only the big cryptos. 

2.  Find the Economic Data

This is seen as an industry-wide challenge because, although plenty of organizations are dedicated to crypto news, it isn't easy to find the real economic data driving the crypto market. The industry's economic system has not been developed, which means the media appears to have free reign in creating greed, fear, and hysteria out of nothing and have nothing solid to back up their claims. You want to steer clear of traps like these, so don't follow a single crypto news organization. Instead, follow several but take everything you read with a pinch of salt. And, as I said earlier in this book, if you hear or read something about a particular crypto, find several other sources that back it up before you take it as gospel. 

3.  Market Regulations

Right now, regulations surrounding cryptocurrency are very new. Although some countries have made large strides ahead, giving their residents good opportunities to get into the market, others haven't. Don't expect things to happen overnight; these things take time. Regulation (or a lack of it) is one of the biggest industry concerns, but it can work in your favor by allowing you to get in when the crypto is at a low price. As more crypto regulation comes in worldwide, those prices are likely to rise, especially when governments and banks accept it as an official financial instrument. 

4.  Hackers

While most people see hacking as a major challenge, you can see it as an opportunity too. Sure, if you get hacked and lose your crypto, it's a massive challenge, but you can take steps to protect yourself from this, i.e., choosing the right exchange, transferring your crypto off the exchange to a secure wallet, and so on. Hacking is unlikely to disappear anytime soon, but the impact on the crypto markets is typically minor, and the resulting negative effects on price are usually only temporary. That's when it becomes an opportunity, though. That's when you can step in and purchase crypto at the lower price, store it in your wallet and wait - the prices will go up again. 

However, the one thing you should never do is automatically invest in the company that got hacked. Make sure you do your research first and look at the circumstances that led to the hack. If an exchange or crypto was hacked in a way that cannot be reversed, there is little point in pouring money into it. However, it can create a knock-on effect on other cryptos for no specific reason other than sharing the industry. When one crypto falls, it tends to affect others too, so do your research before investing your money. 

5.  Crypto Bubbles

The crypto bubble may have burst in 2017, and the crypto markets are settling down and stabilizing. That doesn't mean there won't be another bubble, though, be it a current crypto or a new one. By doing solid and thorough research and analyzing different cryptos, you can learn how to identify where a bubble might occur, usually when a crypto experiences rapid growth in prices with no explicable reason to back it up - basically, market hype is doing its job. That is when you should do one of two things - sell any crypto you may hold at a higher price or steer clear until everything calms down again. That latter part is where the challenge lies, especially when FOMO kicks in. 

6.  Down Markets

One thing that can knock your self-confidence is when your crypto portfolio is down, and it can easily sour a positive mindset. However, please don't allow yourself to think that it's in any way your fault; the markets are constantly on the move, and they rise and fall all the time. See it as an opportunity to buy other good-performing cryptos at a lower price and build up your portfolio without exceeding your risk tolerance. 

None of it means that you are bad at investing and don't know how to make a profit. On the other hand, when your portfolio is up and doing well, it doesn't automatically translate to you being fantastic at what you do and a true master at investing - even experienced investors find their portfolios falling at times. The difference is that they have the experience to weather the storm, which you need to learn. 

7.  New Projects and Cryptocurrencies

There is no denying that new economic systems are building into a huge wave, but there's no telling whether it will culminate in a series of cryptocurrencies backed by a blockchain. one of the more notable projects is called Initiative Q. This began life in 2018 as purely a social experiment, and its economic model is based on one undeniable fact - all currencies have value because people have the currency, and stores accept it as a form of payment. Initiative Q is billing itself as the payment network of tomorrow and is currently experimenting with a currency called Q, which we may or may not see in the future. Early adopters can invite other people and be rewarded with Qs; that encourages those people to then invite even more, and so it goes on. The philosophy behind the project is that the more people have Q, the easier it will be to make it a legitimate currency capable of replacing fiat currency, like the dollar, and then it can be used worldwide.

Projects like this are an opportunity and a challenge. You might stumble over a future model with a high-risk level, or it may not have what you need. For example, Initiative Q was seen by many as nothing more than a pyramid scheme and was instantly rejected out of hand. However, this project contains a low level of risk because you are not required to provide personal information other than your name and a valid email address. According to company policy, the data will be destroyed if the project is not successful. Whether it will or not is a risk you must consider worth taking, but other projects will ask for more personal information, including bank details - do some research, speak to others, and make an informed decision about whether you are prepared to take the risk or not. 

8.  Diversify

Diversification is key to a successful portfolio, but you can always have too much of a good thing. Diversifying too much means spreading yourself thin over multiple assets, and you could find yourself lacking in funds to invest in a top performer when the price is right. Too little investment in big winners means low returns. 

If you have done your homework on a particular crypto and think it will be a winner, consider allocating more of your portfolio to it instead of investing in many smaller ones that may or may not do well. Sometimes, the only way to get a real foothold is to make a couple of investments in bigger cryptos to ensure you reach your goal. 

9.  Letting Emotions Rule Your Investment Decision

When you invest, no matter what it is, you need to make some hard decisions, requiring touch discipline. Sure, you might find a particular crypto quite charming, but you could be doing more harm than good when you let emotion get in the way. If you have invested in a crypto that once performed really well but isn't now, you may need to decide to let it go. Don't hang onto it just because it made you money - if it is failing, it will end up losing you money, and that isn't the idea of investing. Investing is all about profit, so never allow your heart to rule your head - not too much anyway. Yes, gut feeling is good, but relying solely on it can result in financial disaster. 

10.         Use IDDA

Lastly, regardless of the type of trader or investor you are, you should always use IDDA (see chapter 12) to help and guide you. You will need to understand the markets and have plenty of patience, but this strategy will pay off in spades if you put the time in. 

There's no winging it. Just because your favorite movie star promotes a crypto, it doesn't mean you should go ahead and plow all your money into it. Analysis is key. You may believe you have stumbled on a strong trend, but it is only on a single time frame - unless you look for more indicators, you cannot say you have done your technical analysis.

Before you make an investment decision, ensure all the IDDA elements are pointing you the same way.

## Conclusion

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

Thank you for taking the time to read my guide. While you may be well versed in trading and investing in traditional markets, doing the same with digital assets is a whole new ball game. This book aimed to give you the information you needed to get started, including a thorough understanding of what cryptocurrency is, how it works and what blockchain technology is all about.

We also discussed why you would want to put your money into crypto, the advantages and disadvantages of doing so, and some of the different cryptos available to invest in. We talked about crypto mining and looked at short and long-term trading and investing strategies. 

We also looked at two important things – how to minimize your losses while maximizing your gains and taxes and then moved on to the IDDA strategy. With the final two chapters giving you some tips, you should now have all the information you need to hand to get started on your crypto investment journey. 

Before I leave you, here's a word of advice, consider setting up a demo account if you are not confident or have never done any trading before. Most trading platforms offer them, and it gives you the chance to understand how it all works before risking any real money.

Lastly, NEVER invest any more than you can comfortably afford to lose.

Thank you for buying and reading/listening to our book. If you found this book useful/helpful please take a few minutes and leave a review on Amazon.com or Audible.com (if you bought the audio version).

## Introduction

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

Cryptocurrency and Non Fungible Tokens (NFTs) have become a big topic in the past few years. But what are they? Also, how do they work? A quick search on the internet will reveal a lot of information; some of it is useful, most of it garbage. So how can you sift the wheat from the chaff? How do you know you’re not reading some hastily put-together guide that’s only meant to collect your email, push some shady projects on you, and part you from your sweet, sweet dollars? That’s the thing about this space. It is as full of yet unexplored potential as it is rife with too many scams and financial malpractice that has caused many to lose far more than they could afford.

So, dear reader, you’ve made a very wise decision by choosing this book out of all the others. Call it fate or kismet, but this book will spare you from all the fluff and focus on exactly what you need to know to create a powerful, profitable financial portfolio as you invest in this brave new world of NFTs and cryptocurrencies. This space is brand new, and it’s reminiscent of the dot.com era, where if scammers wanted to make a quick buck, all they had to do was slap “.com” on their company name. They’d have unfortunate, uninformed investors flocking toward them in droves, begging them to take their money and run. Back then, there was no way to tell for sure which of the companies would do well and which would tank badly. It’s hard to tell which cryptocurrencies and NFTs are worth your time, especially if you’re new to the space.

Now it could be tempting to assume that just as that dot.com bubble crashed and crashed hard, causing many to lose their “sure bet” investments, the same thing will happen here.
