Take the time to study the way the asset’s price moves each day on a chart, and before you know it, you’ll be able to figure out the driving factors that determine the price and make possibly correct predictions of your own based on past price patterns. When it comes to determining the direction in which the price will move and how far it’s likely to move, you’ll need another form of analysis.

Technical Analysis

Technical analysis is a way of reading and analyzing the market to find trends, patterns, and information that can be used as clues to find profitable trades in cryptocurrency. A lot of traders use technical analysis techniques to help determine what they think the price of a cryptocurrency will do in the future.

You’ll notice that a lot of the same techniques and tactics used in traditional stock analysis are used to interpret cryptocurrency. A lot of the same patterns can be found in both, and many tools and techniques used to analyze stocks are also used to analyze cryptocurrencies. For example, you may have heard people refer to “Fibonacci retracement levels”; financial analysts use this popular technical analysis tool when discussing stock price trends. These are levels that retrace patterns of price movement in a very special way and can be used to predict patterns within price movements. Other technical indicators you could use include moving averages, the relative strength index, and so on.

A technical analysis chart can be used for several things: it can predict things before they happen, like whether a stock price will rise or fall. It can give you an idea of the strength and value of a cryptocurrency before you buy, or it can help you find trading opportunities and signals. Technical traders rely on charts, oscillators, moving averages, momentum indicators, support/resistance levels, and other tools to identify and exploit trends. The most popular types include pattern day trading strategies that take advantage of short-term but more frequent price fluctuations. This type of strategy typically requires strict risk management rules.

Dow Theory in Technical Analysis

Let’s talk about Dow Theory and why it matters when trading cryptocurrency. It’s important to understand where Dow Theory comes from, along with the major principles, in order to understand how it impacts your trading.

The DOW stands for Dow Jones Industrial Average and is an index that tracks average stock prices for a selection of 30 large companies. It was first published in 1896 by American business journalist Charles H Dow. The purpose of this index is similar to many other indices around the world: to track a group of assets that are considered representative of financial markets as a whole. Nowadays, the index is more commonly used for tracking the performance of the US stock market.

Charles H Dow first developed Dow Theory in 1900 in his book The History of Transportation in the United States. He discovered that at a particular historical time, prices behaved as if they had alternatives. He also established that when prices were at an extreme low, there was always a high probability that they would move towards their average value within a short period of time.

Dow kept on experimenting with prices and, based on his findings, created 4 distinct rules of financial markets:

1.  Trend-Following: The market tends to follow a trend. A sustained upward trend (bullish) tends to continue higher, while sustained downtrends (bearish) tend to continue lower. The latter can happen when there isn’t even any actual change in fundamentals.
2.  Panic Braking: We often see great run-ups in the market, usually followed by a short correction that ends up going nowhere and leaves no major impact on the overall trend.
3.  The Shakeout: When the correction is over, the market tends to shake out all remaining weak holders and leaves only those willing to hold on for the long run.
4.  Consolidation: After a short correction, the market proceeds ahead, but this time at a slower pace.

In technical analysis, a trend is a timeframe’s direction of price movements. A bullish trend is when the price moves higher and continues to do so. Conversely, a bearish trend would be when the price moves lower and continues to do so. Trends are considered to present in any market (stocks, bonds, commodities, currencies) where the time value of money plays a role in determining prices. 

Whether or not you think Dow Theory is relevant, it was indeed very influential in financial markets and investments in general. It also has implications for approaching and analyzing cryptocurrency price charts (trading).

In Dow Theory, a swing low or swing high refers to when the price falls or rises below or above a previous session’s ending (daily) price. The shape of these ‘bars’ depends on whether there was an upward or downward movement within the previous session. If the price goes up, it’s called a bullish candle. If the price goes down, it’s called a bearish candle.

In cryptocurrency, the same principles apply. The difference is that movements are usually much more volatile. Therefore the candles will be ‘wider.’ They could also easily go above or below their previous day’s close as there is no daily closing price as such. A swing low then might refer to a swing low in USD value rather than a percentage move from an all-time high (ATH). The same applies to swing highs. If the price goes above a previous day’s candle (high), it’s called a bullish candle, and if it goes below, it’s a bearish candle.

So then, why do we talk about Dow Theory and cryptocurrency trading in the same sentence? Simple: Dow Theory isn’t based on price but rather on movements within prices. As such, you can apply its principles to any asset class across the board. Let me explain how.

Let’s start with the trend-following principle of Dow Theory and how you can use it in your trading. Say you’re looking at a chart that shows a range-bound market (consolidation) coming to a point where a big bearish or downward closing candle appears, breaking below the support line as viewed by the distance between the swing low and swing high (the blue “dashed” horizontal line). This is a bearish divergence which is a reliable sign that we might see some strong downward action.

Support and Resistance, Supply and Demand

Support levels are present at a price where buyers can come in and buy. This is where demand has been consistent with counteracting the supply of sellers. If a stock price falls below these levels, it may be an opportunity to purchase shares within the expectation that it will increase again. Conversely, if the stock price rises too high up to these support levels, there is not enough demand for it currently, and prices are expected to fall again.

![](Images/image00355.jpeg)

Resistance levels are present at prices where sellers can come in and sell. This is where supply has been consistent with counteracting the demand of buyers. If a stock price rallies too high up to these resistance levels, there is not enough supply for it currently, and prices are expected to fall back down again.

A sign of when a support or resistance level will hold is when there is a period between two support levels or two resistance levels known as consolidations. In the case of support levels, consolidations with higher highs and higher lows indicate that supply is decreasing, which signals that the support level will hold. For resistance levels, consolidations with lower highs and lower lows indicate that demand is decreasing, which signals that the resistance level will hold.

Let’s use a real-life example to see how effective this can be. An investor notices that a cryptocurrency has been hovering around $25 for some time without breaking below or above it. If the price falls below $25, support levels can be found by drawing a trendline downwards at each point where it hits $25. This will signify that there is sufficient demand for the crypto’s price to be supported at this level. If you notice that after hovering around $25 without breaking it, there is a consolidation that signifies that supply is decreasing, this shows that the support level will hold.

Similarly, if the crypto’s price rallies above $25, resistance levels can be found by drawing a trendline upwards at each point where it hits $25. This will signify a sufficient supply of shares for the price to be supported at this level. Suppose you notice that after some time of hovering around $25 without breaking it, there is a consolidation which signifies that demand is decreasing. In that case, this shows that the resistance level will hold.

Let’s talk about the concepts of supply and demand in technical analysis of the financial markets. We will discuss the determination of prices in modern economies and the factors that influence supply and demand. It is worth noting that the best-selling eBook from Investopedia, Investing 101: Supply vs. Demand goes into this topic in depth.

The law of supply points to an increase in price as a result of decreases in quantity supplied and/or an increase in price as a result of increases in quantity demanded. Likewise, the law of demand points to an increase in price as a result of decreases in quantity demanded and/or an increase in price as a result of increases in quantity supplied.

In macroeconomic terms, the market supply curve is called aggregate supply, and the market demand curve is called aggregate demand. The intersection of these two curves is known as general equilibrium.

The concept of supply and demand is applied in all price-fixing markets. When a market mechanism determines the price of a commodity, it is said to be in equilibrium. If the quantity supplied equals the quantity demanded, then prices are stable. If there is an excess supply of a commodity, this will cause its price to decline. Likewise, if there is an excess demand for a commodity, this will cause its price to rise.

Supply and demand are some of the most basic concepts that technical analysts can utilize in the financial markets. This is particularly true when determining support and resistance levels.

The principal assumption in technical analysis of price trends is that every chart pattern or price pattern has a beginning, an end, and a reversal during the middle (i.e., during its life). The start of the chart pattern signals the beginning of the trend; its end signals the start of a reversal, and the middle signals a continuation or break of this trend.

Supply and demand analysis is applicable to both long-term and short-term trends. As a general rule, for any trend originating from supply and demand, it is logical to expect the trend to continue until a reversal has taken place. Technical analysts will utilize support and resistance levels in their analysis of price patterns.

Using Technical Analysis to Figure Out the “When”

Where fundamental analysis can give you a general sense of the market’s sentiment about the crypto assets you’re interested in, technical analysis will let you know when the move is likely to occur. Successful trading and investing in this space means you need to be aware of timing. You see, you need technical analysis to track the price action of an asset over a set period if you’re going to get any clues about what’s coming next. Studying the price chart will give you all the information you need, not just about the current and past prices, but about directional bias and trading volume. You can analyze your charts by simply watching the candlesticks or lines on the charts and also using technical indicators to help you if you don’t quite have the hang of reading candles on their own. You’ll have a visual representation of the primary drivers of demand and supply, which is what gives you the price.

The charts may look strange at first, but they’re really no different from a map. You can learn to interpret them the right way and therefore make sure that your decisions are valid. This puts you miles ahead of the guy who overheard Dogecoin was going to pump at the coffee shop and then decided to buy some just as it hit a critical top. When it comes to trading, you need to realize that price is both a driving factor of the action on the chart and the outcome as well. On its own, it’s not the best tool to use to determine what comes next. This is why indicators are useful.

Precision Entries and Exits

When you want to get into a position or a trade, you would be wise to begin humbly by risking only a little at a time and respecting the time frames you’re working with. Keep your sights on small gains and small time frames to get a feel for what it’s like to interact with the markets actively. As you begin to get used to making your own trading decisions for the short term, say based on an hourly chart, you could then decide to start hunting for setups on higher time frames like four hours, eight hours, 12 hours, and even daily and weekly charts. The good thing about pulling back into higher time frames is that you have a higher potential for reward. All the market will require from you is your patience.

If you want to get precise entries and exits, here’s what you need to do:

1.  Look at the charts from the monthly, weekly, and daily perspectives to get a sense of the market.
2.  Drop down to time frames like the four-hour and one-hour charts to help you get a more granular idea of where the market is and whether there are any opportunities for you to be a part of the overall move.
3.  When you spot a setup, you can hop down to the fifteen-minute or five-minute timeframe to patiently stalk your entry. This way, you don’t have to sit through incredible amounts of drawdown and losses from basing your entries on time frames that are too high, which would cause you to jump in before the price is ready to move your way.
4.  To exit your trade, head back up to the higher timeframes and spot logical areas where the price is likely to head. These are spots where the price had hit before and turned around or reacted to in some way. You should make sure your entries are plotted on the higher time frames since these are the premise for your trade to begin with; if not, you’ll be leaving money on the table. If you’re worried about the price not hitting that target, you can scale out of your position by booking profits along your target. That way, if it fails, you still have something to smile at the bank with.

## Chapter 7

 

## The Wonderful World of NFTs

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

Let’s Talk Fungibility

No one keeps all of their money in one savings account. You might have some money at a bank you visit every week, some money at an ATM, and maybe some floating around on your debit card. You use different sources of money for different things, but they’re all the same to everyone else — they’re just numbers on a screen. This is what’s called fungibility, and it’s a key concept in finance.

![](Images/image00356.jpeg)

In a world where money is a commodity, it doesn’t matter where you got it or how you use it. All that matters is what you can buy with it and how much of the stuff you have. In the real world, money is a bit stickier than that — what I mean by that is some money is more fungible than other types of money. Cash, checks, and credit cards are all examples of fungible currency because they’re all just bits in various databases; they’re equally valuable to everyone else at every point in time. However, there are differences between these sources of money as well.

Cash is the most fungible of them all. It’s a direct and traceable link between you and the government that issued it, and, if they so choose, they could track every single penny in existence. Checks are a bit stickier than cash because a check is an IOU from an individual to a bank; your bank might extend that IOU to someone else, but the original check is still sitting in your mom’s checkbook. Credit cards are in a very interesting spot; they’re less traceable than cash, but they’re also less fungible.

The key difference here is that credit cards are more like cash than checks. A credit card allows you to pay for something immediately, but unlike cash, it’s not a direct link between you and the bank. Instead, it’s a link between your bank and the store where you made the purchase, making sending it back to the issuing bank difficult, if not impossible.

This all makes sense from an economic perspective: checks and credit cards are ways of exchanging money that require more red tape for the recipient than for the sender, essentially making them less efficient means of exchange. However, this cost is more than made up for by the convenience of checks and credit cards. Credit cards are more efficient for the merchant because they reduce credit risk, making them easier to accept. Checks have a similar effect on the consumer because they keep track of spending more precisely, which allows you to keep better tabs on your checking account balance.

The moral of the story here is that all money is fungible, but some money is more fungible than others. The difference between perfect and imperfect fungibility matters when it comes to things like liquidity (how easy it is to buy or sell an asset) or information costs (how hard it is to learn about something). With that in mind, let’s talk about NFTs: Non Fungible Tokens.

What Are NFTs?

Non-fungible tokens (NFTs) are digital assets whose attributes (i.e., names of users, security codes, or balances) can’t be changed after being received by the owner. Unlike cryptocurrencies, you can’t create more of them. NFTs are designed to track ownership of digital assets such as data, names, or other assets that can’t be copied and resold. The concept is based on the original idea of fungible and non-fungible assets in economics. While all tokens are fungible (can be considered a homogeneous asset), these non-fungible tokens have the additional characteristics that they can’t be divided into parts (called “fractions”). For example, 1 ETH is indivisible and cannot be broken apart into smaller units; 1 USD, however, is divisible into 100 cents, and so it is fungible.

The recent staggering rise in the popularity and use of cryptocurrencies has created a lot of excitement amongst investors. One of the reasons many people are drawn to cryptocurrency is that they can enter into investment opportunities that they would not have been able to do with traditional fiat exchanges. NFTs (non-fungible tokens) are digital assets that represent something like shares or trademarks, but unlike simple digital assets, these tokens cannot be replicated infinitely. An NFT is ideal for sharing in many cases because it can be easily understood and used as a tool of ownership. While these tokens might not be as widely used as their cryptocurrency counterparts, they are still useful in cryptocurrency ecosystems.

The use of non-fungible tokens (NFTs) is an idea suggested by two professors at the University of California, Berkeley, in a joint paper published in 2016. The goal of these researchers was to create a way to track ownership on the Ethereum blockchain so that individuals could trade or transfer items with one another without the need for a third party like a bank or government to coordinate those transactions. In their paper, the authors proposed that a token could be used to track ownership of digital assets such as data, names, or other assets that can’t be copied and resold. The method suggested by the paper is not yet widely adopted, but that does not mean it cannot have a profound impact on the larger cryptocurrency industry. NFTs were an early idea in the cryptocurrency market, and it is only now that they are gaining more attention.

NFTs are an early idea in the cryptocurrency market, and it is only now that they are gaining more attention. Tokens based on this principle have been used to track ownership of physical property (such as cars or houses) or virtual property like domain names and online game characters. The ability to track ownership of these assets is an important one, especially in the case of digital property, because it allows users to know where their assets are and what they’re doing with them. It also helps prevent theft or illegal use of digital items. These tokens are also useful in solving important problems on the blockchain, as you saw above with the Cryptokitties example. 

Using NFTs to Improve Security

NFTs have a huge potential to improve security across the blockchain. Non-fungible tokens cannot be duplicated infinitely like cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin or Ethereum. For example, in CryptoKitties, users are mostly concerned with the cat’s attributes. Therefore, owners of cats can be sure that they’re the only one who has their cats. However, with cryptocurrencies, there is no way to determine whether someone owns a certain token or not. This can lead to issues where the same asset may be shared across many different addresses and users on the network. With non-fungible tokens, this problem can be prevented because you must have a unique token for each item you own.

In short, you can think of NFTs as almost as a traditional physical bond, but with the added benefit of being completely non-fungible.

NFT Examples

Many other projects provide a way to solve problems using NFTs, but here are a few examples:

Cryptokitties: This is one of the most famous applications built on top of Ethereum. It is a decentralized game where users can buy and sell digital cats. The interesting thing about Cryptokitties’ token is that it does not have any value. They are just representations of the kittens and their attributes (such as color, special powers, or appearance).

Decentraland: Decentraland is a virtual world with its own cryptocurrency (MANA). Its tokens are non-fungible and have different attributes based on the land that they represent. For example, one token may be used to represent a plot of land in a city center, while another may be used to represent land in the middle of nowhere. The interesting thing about these tokens is that they are not transferable between users. For example, it is not possible to sell the token representing the plot of land in the city center and transfer it to someone else.

Rare Pepe Cards: Rare Pepe Cards is another example of how NFTs can improve security across blockchain-based ecosystems. In this project, users can buy a limited number of cards representing rare digital characters. According to the application developers, these cards are “untraceable” because they are not based on the blockchain and have little value. These cards have value because they hold a special purpose; they can be used as utility tokens that can be traded or sold in exchange for other digital assets (like cryptocurrencies). The main idea of this application is to avoid hack attacks and ensure that every single card has a purpose.

For the most part, these tokens exist on the Ethereum blockchain, which allows it to store other kinds of data so that it can do more than the regular ETH coin. You can use this in anything digital, such as art, music, and so on. For the most part, as at the time of this writing, it’s predominantly used in art.

How to Get Started Investing in NFTs

Whether you’re a coder or you’re looking to create your own bespoke NFTr smart contract, there are two things that you’re going to need:

*   Ether is the cryptocurrency for all your transactions. You can buy this from popular exchanges like Coinbase or Luno.
*   A Metamask wallet. You can download this for free. Simply go to metamask.io

Next, you need to check out what the NFT marketplace is like, which means diving deep into websites that host these collectibles. Here are some of the most important ones:

1.  OpenSea: You can find this at https://opensea.io/. This is the biggest marketplace for the peer-to-peer exchange of crypto-collectibles and the first one ever, too.
2.  SuperRare: Find this at https://superrare.com. This market requires you to have an invite before getting on the list.
3.  Axie Marketplace: This one is at https://marketplace.axieinfinity.com/. Here, you can buy and sell all the Axie Infinity game assets.
4.  Alcor: Go to https://alcor.exchange/nft-market. This market is decentralized, allowing one and all to trade NFTs.
5.  Rarible: At https://rarible.com/, you’ll be able to buy NFTs and make your own and put them up for sale.
6.  CryptoPunks Marketplace: https://larvalabs.com/cryptopunks/forsale is where you should head to find punks in pixel forms to buy.
7.  Foundation: Available at https://foundation.app/. This platform lets you mint your NFTs and even auction your creations.
8.  NBA Top Shot: Go to https://nbatopshot.com/ to get NBA moments.
9.  Crypto.com NFT: Find this marketplace at https://crypto.com/nft/marketplace. Mint and auction your NFTs here.
10.                       Binance NFT Marketplace: You can buy and sell NFTs on Binance. Simply visit https://binance.com/en/nft/. Note that while Binance supports Ethereum-based NFTs, it’s a marketplace that primarily runs on the Binance Smart Chain.

Use Case Equals Value

There are multiple ways that NFTs have proven to be useful. This is an undeniable fact when it comes to the creator economy because NFTs allow them to take advantage of the non-fungibility of these tokens to establish ownership rights and origins. Here are some of the ways NFTs can and are being used:

As Digital Art: This is the most popular use case, and little wonder too, as many have made fortunes from creating NFT art and selling to the highest bidders. “Quantum” by Kevin McCoy was the first NFT art minted back in 2014, and it’s also the first time NFTs were used.

As Assets in Gaming: NFTs could be anything in whatever video game you can think of. They could be a world in a gaming universe, unique skins, character outfits, or characters themselves. These kinds of NFTs sell most of any other digitally collectible item.

As Sports Memorabilia: Some people like to collect sports-related NFTs in pretty much the same way the older generations would collect baseball cards, jerseys, and so on. Think of the platform NBA Top Shot, which carries all NBA collectibles that have been digitized.

As Memes: Believe it or not, if you ever become a meme, you could make a lot of money from making an NFT version and selling that. Think of the old memes like Overly Attached Girlfriend or Disaster Girl, and the fact that they and many more have been sold for six figures should get the wheels turning in your head.

As Music: You can find NFTs in this industry as well. For instance, there was the legendary sale of Grimes’ pieces for $6 million on Nifty, among them being a music video titled “Death of the Old,” which went for $388,938, and two shorter videos called ‘Mars’ and “Earth” which went for $7,500 each.

Golden Rules for Investing

As we all know, investors have been flocking to the world of cryptocurrency like never before. While some people are making a fortune, others have been left in the dust — and for a good reason. There are bound to be risks with any investment that has so much potential to make or break you. But by following these four simple steps for investing in NFTs, you can limit your risk and get on the right side of that digital market trend.

Do Your Research: The first step is always research — if you’re new to this world of digital cash, do your own due diligence. Learn about blockchain technology and how cryptocurrencies work in general before diving into a more specific course like Bitcoin or Ethereum. Look at the project you want to invest in; read the white paper, check out the team, and see their roadmap. This will help you decide if the project is worth your investment.

Only Invest What You Can Afford to Lose: This one seems obvious, but it’s one of Wall Street’s most frequently forgotten rules: don’t spend money recklessly just because you can. It’s easy to get caught up in a new trend when everybody else is doing it, but it takes a whole lot more thought and discipline to get ahead. Don’t be the guy who buys 10,000 ETH and watches it lose 50% of its value in the next month — make calculated decisions with your money, and don’t invest more than you can afford to lose.

Only Invest in What You Understand: This one is pretty self-explanatory: don’t buy any NFT that you don’t understand. It’s easy to get swept up in the hype for a new project, but if you don’t know what that project does or how it works, then you’re better off staying out of it. If you’re not confident in your understanding of a digital asset or token, you shouldn’t take part in that specific investment.

Know When to Take a Loss: Any investment will be a risk, even if you do everything right. At some point, the markets will crash, and the value of your digital assets will plummet. You cannot stop this from happening no matter how good you are at investing — but you can prepare for it by knowing when to sell. Identify your “sell point” before investing in a cryptocurrency, and never sell lower than that price. Remember, it’s better to have half of something than all of nothing.

These strategies will help you avoid some common pitfalls for people who invest in NFTs for profit. This won’t guarantee you success, but it will certainly help to mitigate some of the risks — and that’s what finance is all about.

## Chapter 8

 

## How to Buy  
and Sell NFTs for Profit

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

In this chapter, you will learn how to get a slice of the NFT pie. You’ll learn how to buy and sell NFTs and even create your own. With NFTs, if you’re a digital creator, you will find this chapter truly invaluable because you can put up your work online and get paid for it without getting worried about whether someone could try to fake it and sell it.

![](Images/image00357.jpeg)

The Investing Mindset

When it comes to every investment, you need an astute mind and the very best judgment you can make based on all the facts available. This book may give you a head start, but you are the one solely responsible for all your investment decisions, and nothing in here is investment or financial advice.

Having said that, just as with art, NFTs have both objective and subjective value. So, just as with cryptocurrency, there are certain fundamentals you need to keep in mind when you want to make some cool moolah off an NFT drop. In general, as with all investments, you should only put your money into things you really believe in and don’t put in more than you can afford to lose. I seriously can’t stress this enough. Don’t mortgage your life thinking you’re going to make it big by selling the right NFT at the right time. With that said, let’s take a look at some of the important factors you should consider before plunking your cash into any NFT.

How Popular Is the Artist?

This is the key thing that determines whether an NFT that’s about to drop is going to be of any consequence at all. If you have never heard of the artist before, let alone find anything about them on Google, you should pause before investing unless you’re just looking to support new talent. However, if this artist is a household name among NFT enthusiasts, there’s a chance that there are loads of people who would pay a digital arm and leg to have something of theirs. This isn’t always the case, though. For instance, CryptoPunks had no popularity before NFTs were a thing. However, they are traded for thousands of dollars each day on the various NFT marketplaces. This was one of the earlier NFT projects, and for this reason, its value continues to appreciate, along with others like CryptoKitties.

How Secure Is the Blockchain?

If you’re going to be involved in this space, then security has to be at the forefront of your mind. You see, the reason NFTs have become so popular is that they offer incontrovertible evidence of ownership, preventing fraud cases, which has plagued the traditional art world for centuries. Also, they make it possible for you to own something outright and not need some third party to help you validate their existence or keep them safe on your behalf (thanks to the blockchain). It’s no mistake that Ethereum is one of the best networks for maintaining your NFTs securely or creating them too. When you’re looking at an NFT’s blockchain, savvy investors know that it’s important to think about the extent of its decentralization, assuming it’s not based on Ethereum. Another important consideration is whether or not this ledger will have staying power — in other words, how likely is it that this blockchain will continue to be popular decades later?

![](Images/image00358.jpeg)

To be clear, some NFTs don’t exist entirely on the blockchain, or “on-chain,” meaning that the information required for the rendition of the art is stored on the blockchain. The reason for this is that it can be very pricey to host your NFT on a decentralized network and requires PoW. What the artists choose to do instead is to save the image of their NFT using a cloud service — often a third-party one similar to AWS by Amazon, for instance. Some other NFTs are entirely on Ethereum, like Art Blocks, Avastars, and so on. The off-chain NFTs are tokens that demonstrate true ownership but do not keep the art secure on its own.

Flaming Hot Wallets!

Hot wallets make it possible for you to keep, send, and receive all tokens and ETH as well. A good example of one is MetaMask. Every wallet that’s on the internet or web-based is known as a hot wallet. So, where are the cold wallets? In the freezer. Kidding. These are wallets that are entirely offline. They’re basically your hardware wallets, which are much safer because being offline means they can’t be hacked. You have maximum security if you keep your wallet safe and don’t lose it. The smart way to use hot wallets is to make sure you only use them when you’re ready to conduct a transaction immediately, and ideally, you should only use them for minuscule transactions that wouldn’t hurt you too much if things go awry.

While MetaMask hasn’t been hacked, there have been scammers who have been tricking people into giving them access to their wallets and cleaning them out. All you ever have to worry about, for the most part, are phishing attacks. So please never give away sensitive information about your wallets to anyone, for whatever reason. Your wallet’s passphrase and password are yours and yours alone. Stay away from emails with suspicious-looking links, even from people you’ve been in touch with before because they could have had their accounts hacked. Never click on pop-up ads, and stay away from weird websites with too many ads and pop-ups.

The interesting thing about MetaMask is that it is an HD wallet — meaning a hierarchical deterministic wallet. In simple English, it helps you back up everything connected to your wallet by creating a hierarchical structure reminiscent of a tree. This has public and private addresses as branches, ensuring there’s no reason to create pairs manually to keep all the data safe. Another good thing about this hot wallet is that you can control your online identity whenever you need to create or manage a transaction on Ethereum with a DApp. Having lauded its amazing features, please never put too much in your wallet. After all, you wouldn’t carry around your life savings in your back pocket. You should only have what you need and nothing more. You should get a hardware wallet, like the durable Ledger or a Trezor, and please make sure you buy your wallets from the manufacturing company and not a third-party website — that’s right, not even Amazon. There have been some compromised wallets sold on that site, and as far as I know, CEO, entrepreneur Jeffrey Bezos hasn’t saved any unfortunate buyers from getting hacked.

Setting Up Your MetaMask

To use MetaMask, you have to install it onto your browser. It’s set up to be pretty easy to use, and it integrates seamlessly with OpenSea, and other websites based on Web 3.0, which is basically a framework that makes the internet even more connected than ever. MetaMask is your gateway to the Ethereum blockchain and allows you to easily manage your account. It’s a wallet for storing all ERC20 tokens, including your ETC, and will allow you to work with all websites that can connect with MetaMask, meaning you don’t need to have a full Ethereum node to make use of your DApps.

You can install MetaMask on Firefox, Chrome, and others as well. To install MetaMask, simply head over to https://metamask.io, hit “Download Now,” and this will take you to your browser’s store where you can add the extension to your browser by simply clicking Add or Allow or whatever pops up on your browser to that effect. When you’re done, you should see the extension towards the top right-hand corner, and all you have to do is click on it to open it.

After this, click on the Get Started button, click Agree when asked to share data with MetaMask, and then enter a password that’s unique and secure and store it in multiple safe locations online and offline. It’s advisable to make sure your password is alphanumeric with some special characters thrown in. After this, you’ll get a passphrase that has 12 words, which will allow you to recover your account should you forget your password or something. You need to know that there’s no such thing as customer service, so if you lose this passphrase, there’s really no way for you to recover your account. It’s best to store this passphrase safely offline and online. You’ll be prompted to enter this passphrase in the correct order, so do that, and then click on All Done.

Using Your MetaMask Wallet to Buy Ether

You can buy Ether using your MetaMask in small quantities, but if you want to buy a lot of Ether, you’re going to have to submit know-your-customer documentation to Wyre, which is the payment vendor for Ethereum that will let you buy your crypto with your card, or if you prefer, with Apple Pay. Here’s how to buy your precious Ether:

*   Click on the MetaMask icon on your browser.
*   Sign in to your wallet by entering the right username and password.
*   Click on the Buy button.
*   Click “Continue to Wyre,” and this will open up a new window with the address https://pay.sendwyre.com/purchase
*   Choose your payment method and enter how much you intend to buy.

The tokens will be in your MetaMask wallet when you’ve bought your Ether. If you prefer, you can buy your Ether with Coinbase. It’s easy to set up an account with Coinbase by following all the steps. Just head over to coinbase.com to get started. When you buy the Ether, you can transfer it from your Coinbase wallet to your Ether wallet, so you can now buy your NFT.

![](Images/image00359.jpeg)

The Gateway to NFTs

Nifty Gateway is the creme-de-la-creme of digital art platforms for NFTs. The Winklevoss twins of Facebook fame bought this platform from Griffin and Duncan Cock Foster — also twins. This platform has sold legendary pieces from Grimes, Beeple, and more phenomenal artists, and its connection with Sotheby’s makes it one of the go-to platforms for listing, buying, and selling NFTs.

Keep in mind that Nifty Gateway refers to its rare NFT drops as Nifties, which are the same thing as NFTs and have the same ERC-721 format. You can make your purchases with your card on Nifty Gateway or use your Ether. You can also buy NFTs from crypto games on this platform.

This platform is worth looking into because there are usually drops, which are limited releases of art by artists who usually have highly coveted work. Note that these drops usually sell out in a matter of minutes, so the fastest fingers are the happiest ones. If you can get your hands on these drops, you can resell them for much higher prices on reseller platforms such as StubHub. You could just buy them from the owners on sites like this if you missed out. Here’s how to set up your Nifty Gateway account:

1.  Head over to https://niftygateway.com
2.  Hit the Sign-Up button if you don’t have an account, or Sign In if you already do.
3.  If you’re signing up, fill out all the details required.
4.  Check your email for a confirmation message.
5.  Copy the code you get and paste it into the relevant box.

Now you have a verified Nifty Gateway account, it’s time to check out which NFTs to buy. Here’s how:

1.  Head over to https://niftygateway.com.
2.  Click on Drops. If you’re already signed in, you should see the upcoming drops. Click on any of them to see more information.
3.  If you see something you want to have, you can click on the Bid button at the bottom of the image. You can either buy with your card or with ETH.

Please be mindful about spending money on NFTs because there’s a chance that the NFT you buy will decline in value with time. This is why it’s important to only spend money on the NFTs you actually like.

An Open Sea of NFTs

You can buy your NFTs on OpenSea if you’ve got ETH or BTC and your MetaMask is ready. Simply head over to https://opensea.io, then log in to MetaMask. You can surf OpenSea by checking out the Marketplace, which has a bunch of categories, including what’s trending, the newly minted tokens, and the recent listings. Here’s how to buy your NFTs on OpenSea:

Hop Over to the Marketplace and Choose Art: This should take you to the art page with the most recent NFT art. Looking for something more specific? Use the search function.

Click on the Image of What You’re Interested In: This leads you to the main page of the art, where you can look at the trading history and other relevant information. You’ll know how much has previously been paid for this NFT, who owned it, who created it, the blockchain securing it, the storage locations of the art, and so on.

Hit Buy Now: This will open up a transaction page so you can purchase it and add it to your wallet. Once you’re done, it will be connected to your wallet.

Making Your Own NFT

The process of creating your own NFT is very easy, and you’re about to find out how so. Use any software you want to create your own art. A popular one is Sketch, which you can find on https://sketch.io. The great thing about OpenSea is the wide variety of image, audio, and video formats you can upload. The recommendation is to make sure your file size isn’t larger than 20MB, but it can accept uploads of files as large as 40MB. It supports the following formats:

*   WAV
*   OGG
*   MP3
*   MP4
*   WEBM
*   PNG
*   JPG
*   GIF
*   GLTF
*   GLB
*   SVG

After creating your art, you can save it to any of these formats and save it to your computer so you can use that as an NFT. Now it’s time for you to create your collection. Here’s what you need to do:

1.  Head over to https://opensea.io.
2.  Click on your profile and select My Collections. If you already have your MetaMask setup, this can take you right to OpenSea on its own.
3.  Log into your MetaMask, which will be your unique identifier on OpenSea.
4.  Choose “Create a Collection.”
5.  Name your collection and put in a description.

Now it’s time to add your collection:

1.  Select Add item from the collection page. You’ll be prompted to upload your art.
2.  You can drag and drop your art into the specified box.
3.  Fill in your description and whatever else you want to add.
4.  Hit the Create button.

Now you have an NFT. Share it on social media and promote it however else you can.

Rarible

Alexander Salnikov and Alexei Falin created this decentralized marketplace, and you can trade your NFFTs here. It’s’ one of the most popular marketplaces because of the diversity in functionality and the fact that it cares about artists. You can put up files with high resolution create smart contracts to receive royalties even after selling your art, each and every time your art is traded. That’s recurring, passive income for life unless the NFT is traded on a platform that doesn’t have that system of paying royalties in place.

Rarible is led by the DAO, which, as you recall, means it’s decentralized, and there’s no central ruling body. The community that runs the place is made up of NFT enthusiasts and artists that are in charge of keeping the platform running. When you’re part of the community, you have a say in how the platform evolves through the token for this platform, called a RARI. This is basically a governance token that allows you to vote on the changes and upgrades that should occur. The only way to get RARI is not by buying it but by participating in various activities on the platform, either as a seller, buyer, or creator. The RARI token is distributed based on your activity level each week.

It’s super easy to create your own NFT on this site, and you’re going to love the simplicity of the interface when you have to publish your art and create smart contracts that give you royalties and more. It’s important to note that this is not a perfect site because some unscrupulous individuals will pay for their own art to get their hands on some RARI. Anyway, here’s how to get started on Rarible.

First, head over to https://rarible.com, and then hit the Connect Wallet button. Choose the correct wallet, and then once you’re signed in, that means you’re also connected to Rarible. Next, head over to the Settings page and enter your display name. You can link your socials to your rarible account if you want to. To save the changes you’ve made, you should click on the Update Profile button, and this will prompt your MetaMask wallet to give you a pop-up. Follow all the instructions. Note that any pending transaction will stop you from updating any information until you handle the transaction or clear it out.

Royalties on Rarible

When you want to create an auction that has royalty, the first thing is you must have your artwork ready. Then go to the Rarible website and sign in to MetaMask. Hit the Create button choose the Multiple options under Create Collectible. Select your file, upload it, and it will be ready for you to click on Open for Bids so others can start bidding. After that, choose Rari from the Choose Collection section, input the title, description, percentage of royalties, and the number of copies, then you can create the item, head back to your Metamask, and hit Approve. If there’s no money in your MetaMask, you will have to fix that by funding it. Refresh the page, and you’ll see the NFT there.

## Chapter 9

 

## NFTs — Investing Like a Pro

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

First, you should note that cryptocurrency isn’t the same as NFTs, even though they’re both recorded on the blockchain. Cryptocurrency is fungible, but NFTs aren’t. In other words, a Bitcoin’s a Bitcoin, but you can’t swap out a CryptoKitty for a CryptoPunk. You can’t even replace one CryptoPunk with another because that’s not how that works regarding fungibility. Every NFT has unique traits and values, which can change independently without needing any correlation to other NFTs on the market. As long as someone wants to buy and someone else wants to sell, the NFT has some value. Some think there’s a chance the market for NFTs will crash and that we’re dangerously close to a bubble. In part, this is because too many celebrities and social media influencers are scamming their followers and doing rug pulls on them these days.

![](Images/image00360.jpeg)

Before You Invest

Ever since cryptocurrencies and NFTs, investment hasn’t been the same. These technologies have made it even easier for the average person to consider other ways to invest and grow their money. As at the time of this writing, the space is in desperate need of regulations to protect all parties involved. In the past, with old school traditional investing, there was a time anyone could take part, and while this made many rich, many more were left economically devastated around the 1920s, and this led to the establishment of the Securities and Exchange Commission or the SEC in the year 1934, to make sure all investors are protected and that the rules are followed at all times. This was a very vital part of the fight against the devastating havoc wreaked on the people by the Great Depression so that there would be no recurrence of the mess.

Another thing meant to protect investors is the 1933 Securities Act which means that all securities sales have to be registered, as long as they’re being sold in the United States or to US citizens. This empowered the body called the Federal Trade Commission to put the kibosh on the sales of fraudulent securities and jail offending parties.

Why all this talk about the SEC and the FTC? Because it’s important to note that everything about the blockchain, including the digital and actual documents, is all under the purview of these bodies. So, as you check out various NFTs, you need to realize attendant laws are depending on your country of origin and the country of the person you’re dealing with. These bodies consider NFTs in the same way as the traditional investment assets to figure out whether or not they can be classified as securities. As mentioned, many have been defrauded in this space, and there is speculation that NFTs are really just a vehicle for money laundering schemes. Regulation of the space will take a fair bit of time to start coming together, and enforcing the regulations will take even more time, so you must stay safe.

So, Consider This: Let’s say you purchase an NFT that has ties to an asset, and it’s sold to you as a part of a collectible that has a very clear and accessible record that shows it’s the real deal on the blockchain. It’s very unlikely to be considered as financial security. It’s really just like buying some art in real life. However, there are so many traits that NFTs can have which could easily class them under financial instruments, such as when you buy an NFT that’s supposed to give you ROI (Return on Investment). So, when you’re checking out an NFT, and you’re not sure whether it’s an investment contract, think about whether you need to put money into it, it’s considered a common enterprise, and is meant to yield you a fair bit of profit. If so, is that profit you’re getting on account of other people’s work? These four points, if affirmative, mean you should pay attention to the FTC and SEC rules.

Should You Invest in NFTs?

This is a very important question you should ask yourself before you get involved so that you don’t get into trouble or feel disappointed because your expectations weren’t met. You can invest in other things that have more clarity and history, are easier to predict, and have more available data for you to research. Also, even if you choose to invest in something else, you should still never invest more than you’re willing to lose to remain financially afloat if things don’t work out as expected. I feel this is a good time to remind you that there’s a chance no one will want to buy the NFTs you buy or mint, which is why you need to make sure that you’re only putting your money behind the art you really do love owning and artists you believe in so that it never feels like you made a loss.

When people dip their toes into NFT investing, it’s usually because they are interested in being the very first person to own a specific NFT and want bragging rights, or because they want to make some money from reselling the stuff that they own. However, there’s not enough information, especially from the secondary markets, for you to be more certain of what you’re doing, even though there are many rags to riches stories in this space.

This moment in time for NFTs is eerily similar to the heyday of Bitcoin when everyone was making a pile of money from buying it and other altcoins. Everyone else wanted to hop on the gravy train and did just that, losing their money because the bubble had popped. Always have this in the back of your mind when you’re investing any money into this space.

Strategies for NFT Investing

Some NFTs could be the proverbial golden goose for you and others that may lose their value rather quickly. This is a risky space, and the only way to stay safe is to get all the information that you can on each piece unless you’re in it more for the fun of being a collector, in which case you can just have fun never selling. If your goal is actually to make money, though, you must DYOR — Do Your Own Research. If you just begin investing willy nilly without informing yourself adequately, you’re asking to get burned. It would be just like spending an arm and a leg on a traditional piece of art that really has no value. So, let’s address the all-important first step: NFT valuation.

How to Value Your NFT

Your NFT has value because it’s wanted and scarce. That’s just the same as baseball cards, stamps, and other traditional collectibles. It only stands to reason that the more people want something, and the harder it is to get it, the more valuable it’s going to be because people will want to pay more to obtain it. If no one wants to buy, there’s no point in having an NFT. It doesn’t matter if an NFT is the only one that exists. If no one wants it, then it’s value-less.

Note that NFTs are different from traditional collectibles in the sense that the NFT is really just a record on the ledger that shows proof and ownership, so it doesn’t have the digital content it represents because the blockchain isn’t designed to work that way, and it would be very expensive to store large file sizes on the regular blockchains. However, the tech behind NFTs can help with creating value. Since scarcity and desirability equal value, it can be quite problematic to collect digital items because you could always just “right click and save,” and your copy would still have the same quality. NFTs can address this issue by making it possible for the artists to have limited copies of their art, or if they want, to create original tokens and issue those rights to whoever is willing to pay the price. So NFTs can stand in for the asset’s actual value. How do you figure out the value of an NFT before you buy them? There are several different ways, but generally, it’s all about the market, the brand, and the scarcity of the asset in question.

When it comes to the market, we’re talking about where the pieces are sold. So, an investor can look at the other transactions on the market to see the various price points of items by the same artist or by other artists with similar styles and genres, which could inform the value of the NFT itself. Think of it like trying to figure out the baseline or average of prices for real estate in a specific neighborhood so you know if you’re getting ripped off or getting a fair offer.

As for the brand, it’s all about the person who created the NFT. For instance, you can buy some of the priciest NFTs on NBA Top Shot because you have celebrity basketballers involved, like LeBron James. Naturally, you can expect a lot of value when it comes to anything tied to LeBron James. In the same way, you can expect anything sold by Beeple will be worth several tons of the coin. I’d go so far as to say Beeple could just put a black dot against a white surface, and it would sell for millions simply because of the brand and not the art itself. So, you could buy NFTs that aren’t connected to a well-known brand, but in that case, it might be hard for you to get actual value from them in the long term unless they become popular over time.

![](Images/image00361.jpeg)

Finally, there’s the matter of scarcity. When a popular artist like Grimes puts out just one copy of their work, it will have a lot more value than if there are 5,000 copies of the work available. This is why the marketplaces you visit are very clear about letting you know how many copies of an art collectible will be dropped because that gives investors an idea of the value of the art. It’s also a great way for artists to make sure their work has value, as they can adjust how many copies will be made available to the public, which will automatically help their bottom line.

What’s Your Strategy?

When you’re absolutely certain that you would like to put your money into NFTs, the first thing that you’ve got to do is research. The moment you decide to do this, you realize that so much is going on. It’s easy for you to be overwhelmed with the sheer amount of information on what’s valuable and what isn’t. There are literally millions of NFTs out there that are available on several marketplaces that make it incredibly difficult for you to concentrate your search on one particular area.

As part of your strategy, you want to make absolutely sure that the marketplace you have chosen is a legitimate one. On top of that, the underlying asset you’re looking at has to be the real deal. I’m so particular about authenticity because it’s easy for anyone to create their own NFT and claim it’s an actual original when it really isn’t. Because NFTs and the Ledger they exist on is decentralized, it makes it extremely difficult for you to figure out the actual reputation of the marketplace you’re dealing with and the quality of the NFTs themselves.

Since the sheer number of NFTS out there can be pretty overwhelming, it’s a smart idea, to begin with, a genre that you are particular about. After all, if it’s something that you actually love, it should be fun for you to do your research on it. Also, to be clear, just because you’ve made a purchase does not mean you should stop keeping up with the market. You must continue to read and stay up to date on all developments regarding that NFT. This way, you’ll know when the right time is to sell, and you’re not left behind holding the bag after everyone else has dumped theirs.

Chances are, the more you learn about the market, the more you’ll be willing to invest in it. At this point, it is a very good idea for you to have your goals in mind and be very clear about them. You must make sure that these goals are very clearly defined and written out, where you can see them every day so that you continue to deliberately stick to the plan.

A key part of your NFT investing strategy should be the trading volume, which is a reference to the number of buyers and sellers that are participating in the market at any given point in time. This would not be a problem if you’re talking about any other asset class like cryptocurrency or CFDs. But when it comes to NFTs, the thing is, the market is usually pretty thin, meaning there are not a lot of buyers and sellers. Scammers and other unscrupulous players in the NFT space are aware of this, so they create a bunch of accounts and continue to buy and sell their assets repeatedly to make it seem like they have value when they actually don’t. The constant buying and reselling create a false value that fools other investors into thinking this might be worth putting their money into.

When it comes to researching NFTs you’d like to invest in, you need to check out sources like Twitter and Reddit. However, you should also be aware that bots and fake accounts could make it seem like an NFT is really hot when it’s not. It’s not too difficult for scammers who want to run a pump and dump to have a bunch of these fake posts showing up on these sites to encourage people to funnel funds into the “investment.”

You should also consider having a specific target of your profits for your strategy. It’s pretty much the same thing as when investing in cryptocurrency. There should be a set price point at which you will sell no matter what. If you continue to flout your target, this could work out for you once or twice, but it could really come back to bite you in the you-know-where when it doesn’t. The target you choose should be based on the research you’ve done on that particular asset, and it needs to be reasonable. A very good rule of thumb is to “be greedy when they’re fearful and fearful when they’re greedy.” After all, why would you buy anything at a premium when you could buy it at a discount? Or why would you sell anything at a discount when you could sell at a premium? The name of the game is to buy low and sell high. Keep that in mind, and you’ll be just fine.

What does it really mean to buy low and sell high anyway? If you wait for absolute rock bottom prices before you buy, there’s a chance that you will miss out on a good dip. The same thing goes for selling. Let’s say that the price of an NFT is going to hit $10,000. You want to do the smart thing and get out at $8500 or $9000. No one will give you an award for holding on to the very top. You’re not in this for the accolades. You’re in this to make some money, so don’t get greedy and try to grab everything on the table, because otherwise, the market will take everything back from you and then some. 

How to Spot the Hotties

Again, people have different reasons for getting involved in NFTs. For some people, it’s purely all about the art, and for others, it’s just a form of social signaling to let everyone else know that because they own a particular NFT, they are someone to be recognized.

### On Taxes

Now, we will talk about how to report taxable gains from exchanging one type of property for another. This information is not just helpful for tax season— it will help you identify strategies to minimize the impact of capital gains tax in the future.

To do so, we will focus on a very specific part of this process: reporting and paying taxes on exchange-traded tokens (NFTs). We’ll start by looking at why these digital assets can sometimes be viewed as similar to stocks or other securities, which means they may earn income when they’re sold. Next, we’ll explore how to track and report this capital gains income related to your digital assets. And finally, we’ll touch briefly on the maximum amount of capital gains you can deduct each year.

Why do NFTs sometimes get viewed as securities? Let’s start with some background. In terms of “securities,” the U.S. government are referring to a specific classification of property that may be a good fit for taxation — depending on how it’s being used and how it’s being sold.

The IRS classifies NFTs as “property” for federal tax purposes. The term “property” refers to a bundle of rights (such as the right to receive digital currency) that are owned by the person holding the title. For example, you might have a property right when you store an Amazon digital gift card in your digital wallet. In this case, you own these rights, and they’re yours to sell or trade (or not) at any time. In fact, an Amazon gift card is just one type of NFT created to facilitate purchases, sales, and other transactions — with each one has its own unique set of rules surrounding how it’s used and exchanged.

The IRS has a specific set of rules in place for “securities,” which are property that can include debt and equity interests in an enterprise. This includes shares of stock, bonds, investment contracts, notes, and more. The IRS uses the term “security” when referring to these types of property because they’re instruments that represent the rights to own or control something else. So, does this mean NFTs (which are considered property) are securities for tax purposes? The answer isn’t so simple — it depends on how they’re used.

### The Howey Test

The IRS uses a set of criteria, known as “the Howey test,” to determine whether or not an investment is considered a security. If an investment meets these requirements, which include transferability and the expectation of profit from others, then it will likely be taxed as security. The Supreme Court of the United States has applied this definition ever since it heard a commercial dispute in 1946 involving dealings with citrus groves. As such, the term “Howey test” often refers to the Supreme Court’s definition for what qualifies as security (even though this test is used today by regulators and agencies beyond just the IRS).

The Howey test is kind of complicated, so let’s break it down. The key question to ask is whether or not you have a “common enterprise.” If you do, then the IRS will likely consider these NFTs to be securities, and they will be taxed as such. Let’s look at Amazon gift cards as an example. If you own digital gift cards that can be used to make purchases on the Amazon marketplace, then it’s likely going to count as security. The reason? These NFTs are specifically created for trading purposes (with each card is assigned its own unique identifier), and there’s an expectation that investors will profit from them — this is why they’re deemed securities by the U.S. enforcement agencies.

However, this doesn’t mean that the IRS will automatically consider every type of digital asset to be a security if it meets the following requirements: You can create, transfer, and store them on a blockchain (similar to how Bitcoin works). You expect profit from others when you sell or trade them. You can do this without transferring any value (or shares) from yourself to someone else. These are the four steps the IRS has established as part of their “Howey test” for determining if an asset is a security.

Note that in its ruling in the case of Northern Pipeline Construction Co. v. Marathon Pipe Line Co., the Supreme Court of the United States discussed potential exceptions to these four criteria (as mentioned above). If there are circumstances where an asset should be treated differently, it’s likely that it will meet these requirements.

Moving on — does this mean that all NFTs are securities? This depends on what you’re using them for. For example, a digital token used to fuel a program such as Ethereum’s decentralized blockchain could potentially be considered a security by the IRS because those rights can be transferred or sold (or traded) in the same way that shares of stock often do.

How can you track and report NFTs as securities? Here’s an important thing to note: If these digital assets are securities, then when you sell one of these tokens or sell your rights to someone else, they’re treated as a capital gain — depending on whether this is a “short-term” or “long-term” sale. That means that capital gains on securities are taxed at the federal level on the day of sale. As a result, most NFT transactions are either bought or held for less than 12 months. Therefore, short-term capital gains are taxed at your federal income tax rate.

Capital gains on securities are taxed at the federal level on the day of sale. As a result, most NFT transactions are either bought or held for less than 12 months. Therefore, short-term capital gains are taxed at your federal income tax rate. Long-term capital gains on securities only apply to assets you’ve held for more than 12 months (from the day you bought them). These long-term gain rates can vary depending on how much money you’re making, but they can generally be as low as 15%.

Should you consider selling your NFTs as securities? The short answer is “it depends.” If you use these digital assets for investment purposes, the IRS will likely consider these assets as securities. For example, suppose you trade an asset for another asset in an attempt to make a profit. In that case, that’s essentially a transfer of ownership, and it could be considered a security transaction. The same applies if you sell your rights to someone else — that could be considered a sale of securities (and by the way, it’s important to note that this applies regardless of whether or not you’re paid in cryptocurrency).

However, it’s important to note that the IRS has determined that NFTs are not considered securities because most don’t fit the definition. As such, if you’re using a digital asset for personal purposes and not trading it as part of a business venture, then you’re likely not going to be taxed on what you earn from it.

The IRS also notes that they have rules to determine whether or not an asset is considered a security. For example, if “securities” (such as shares in an enterprise) are created or transferred to obtain benefits with certainty or profit from others, they will be treated as a security. If you and others participate in an activity that’s similar to the NFTs, then it’s likely going to be treated as a security. If you sell or transfer one of these tokens (or your rights with it) to benefit from them, it will be considered a securities sale.

If you own NFTs and have the final say over which way they’ll move (other people can’t change them), you could be considered an owner of a security. However, if others have control over the asset and don’t receive profits from these decisions (and instead pay for them), they won’t be treated as a security.

As a general rule of thumb, if you’re using NFTs for personal or non-commercial purposes, the IRS will likely not treat these events as security sales. This is one reason why many people in the crypto community believe that digital assets are not investments.

However, this doesn’t mean that you should ignore possible capital gains taxes. For example, suppose you own a type of digital asset which is classified as stock by Coinbase (and other exchanges). In that case, they’ll report every transaction as a “Form 1099-B” to the IRS and send out copies to all investors involved in that transaction.

Note: The IRS has not yet declared its stance on how they’re going to treat NFTs. Their ruling noted that they were bound to follow the rules and regulations set forth by Congress. Therefore, while they believe that the above criteria will be used to determine whether or not NFTs are securities, this doesn’t mean that you shouldn’t report them as such.

What’s Next for NFTs?

The short answer is that we’re still waiting for congressional action (which could take many years). However, as a result of several tax bills which recently passed through Congress, it’s unlikely that there will be a rush for action.

In their ruling, the IRS noted that Congress dealt with this issue previously and set criteria for them to follow. Even if it takes many years for a final decision to be made, it appears as if the IRS intends to take its time and study the situation further.

The long answer is that there’s still plenty of room for improvement in regard to NFTs. For example, we’re not entirely sure how we’ll deal with these digital assets and how they’ll work once they’re part of an exchange such as Coinbase or other sites (since they’re currently being used as collectibles).

Even if the IRS doesn’t decide on their status as securities, they’ll likely take the time to make some important decisions. For example, NFTs are treated as assets and not investments right now. When you buy or sell them on an exchange or use them as a currency (rather than being held in a digital wallet), there’s not much regulation to govern these types of transactions.

As such, we could see this crypto space expanding even more in the coming months and years with regard to regulations. There are even a few bills out there that could change how digital assets are treated, including one Senate bill (S.2234) proposed by Senator Mike Rounds, which aims to make NFTs taxable property.

Regardless of how long it takes for them to be defined by the IRS or how they’ll deal with these events moving forward, it’s likely that they’ll play a role in the development of NFTs. As a result, it’s important to understand how they will be treated before you use them or sell them on an exchange.

## Chapter 10

 

## How to Spot Scams

![](Images/image00317.jpeg)

Spotting Crypto Scams

Cryptocurrency scams are one of the many ways criminals seek to take advantage of unsuspecting people today. People should always be aware and continue to learn how to spot cryptocurrency scams in 2022.

Scammers have been using social promotion techniques to spread scams globally over the past few years. Good news always gets retweeted and friend-of-a-friend and shared on social media networks which scammers can use to trick unsuspecting people into believing that their investment opportunity is legitimate. Here are some examples of how fraudulent cryptocurrency promotions are spreading on social networks:

These scams seem to be working as people start sharing their supposed success stories with family and friends. Scammers then use strategies like fake testimonials that were also seen in the past as part of pyramid schemes.

![](Images/image00362.jpeg)

Do you have a friend or coworker who is hanging out in cryptocurrency forums, social media sites, and message boards? If not, that’s okay. Be sure to keep an eye on your loved ones and if you see anything that seems odd with their behavior, tell them right away to reach out to you or the authorities.

If you’re inquiring about cryptocurrency investment opportunities with people that you don’t know, it’s best to be cautious. Check the background of anyone who is contacting you. A simple Google search will help identify if someone is associated with any financial services companies or if they have been involved in previous scams before.

One other thing that should be kept in mind is that you shouldn’t send money to anyone without verifying the legitimacy of their website. Use the websites of legitimate businesses to see if they are offering any incentives to invest in cryptocurrency.

For people who are spending most of their time online, their online profiles can also be used by scammers. If you’re a crypto enthusiast, make sure that you visit your Facebook, Twitter, and LinkedIn accounts and make sure that they are fully updated with all the information needed. If any suspicious messages or accounts seem odd, contact your social media manager right away and have them run a background check on them.

While most people may see this as a new trend, people are still being scammed from social promotion techniques. Here are some examples of how it has been happening in the past few years: On July 31, 2017, the SEC revealed that it was investigating cryptocurrency investment scams instigated by a man calling himself Dave Hamilton. The SEC labeled this as an “illegal ICO.”

Then on November 8, 2017, Bitcoin.com was accused of being a scam after receiving complaints from multiple users who claimed that they had not received their paid-for support in response to support emails sent to them by the Bitcoin.com customer service department.

In April 2018, a University of Technology Sydney (UTS) study showed that up to $1 billion was lost by investors because of fraudulent cryptocurrency promotions. The report also reported that around $2.6 billion had been invested into cryptocurrencies in 2017, and the number was expected to increase exponentially.

The most recent scam case occurred on June 25, 2018, when a fake Instagram account was created called @cryptotalerts with over 3 million followers. The account posted various fake news stories with “real” and “fake” snippets at the top. The post was believed by many to be real until it was discovered that the account had been created on June 4, 2018. While some of the fake accounts are posting full stories, some have been exclusively posting fake news articles using “real” headlines from global news media outlets such as USA Today and The Guardian to create an illusion that these news stories had been posted earlier. As cryptocurrency gains a steady presence in our everyday lives, the number of people who are looking to invest in it will continue to rise. Therefore, people need to be aware of how they can spot cryptocurrency scams in.

No centralized body regulates the cryptocurrency industry, and its market value is determined solely by demand and supply. A lot of good things are happening as well as bad things with this new industry, so caution should always be exercised when considering investing with anyone, even if they seem legitimate at first glance. The best thing you can do for yourself continues to learn about bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies to prevent yourself from being a victim of a scam.

Ten Tips for Avoiding Crypto Scams

1.  Before investing, make sure that you understand what Bitcoin mining is and whether you have the right hardware to take part in it. Bitcoin mining is the process of verifying and adding new blocks to the Blockchain to maintain the Bitcoin network. This process requires specialized hardware and software setup, which can be expensive, so it’s very important to be aware of what you need before investing.
2.  If you’re looking for a miner, then it’s probably best not to buy pre-assembled rigs yourself. When buying one that’s assembled, there are quite a few things you need to consider and then confirm which parts are included or required.
3.  Another option is to buy a kit of parts and assemble it yourself. This is best done with the help of a friend with some knowledge of the mechanics of online tutorials and available videos.
4.  Some people will try to convince you that cloud mining services can provide you with a great ROI (return on investment). Cloud mining refers to a service where the miner rents their dedicated machine from the cloud rather than from their own personal or home computer. Unfortunately, cloud mining does not currently have a reliable track record for performance, so it should be avoided.
5.  It is best not to purchase any mining contracts when buying cryptocurrency. Mining is a difficult and time-consuming process, so knowing exactly what you’re purchasing before investing is important.
6.  Claiming that mining success is guaranteed in a certain timeframe (like 2 weeks), or for life, or for unlimited periods of time, are all red flags that should raise suspicion about an investment opportunity that may yield no return. If the claim is made, there should be some kind of guarantee or significant investment required to validate the claim.
7.  Another form of scamming involves random chat groups set up on Telegram, Slack, BBM, etc. The scammer poses as a trustworthy person who can help you make profits from your mining rigs. The scammer will also ask for a percentage of the profits, usually about 20% to 50%.
8.  Yet another type of cryptocurrency scam is similar to the second type and involves a chat group. This time, the difference is that instead of making a promise about how much you can earn, the person will tell you to invest in cryptocurrency and how much to buy. Then they’ll ask for some percentage of your investment as commission.
9.  To avoid being scammed, it is important to be aware of how the scams work. This will increase your ability to identify them and avoid falling for them.
10.                       Another step you can take is to join online communities where other people discuss which exchanges and trading platforms are trustworthy and legitimate. Since most people only remember the biggest scandals, these communities will help you discover lesser-known scams that you might otherwise fall victim to.

Always be on the lookout for investment opportunities that seem too good to be true because they probably are. The best cryptocurrency scams claim that they’ll earn you a lot more money than other investments, but always with little or no risk involved. If it sounds too good to be true, then it is.

Avoiding NFT Scams

As the cryptocurrency market continues to grow, so are the number of scams and frauds that target users. Unfortunately, scammers have become more sophisticated than ever in their tactics for stealing your money - but with a little education, you’ll avoid getting involved in one of these potentially embarrassing situations. As such, we’re going to look at your red flags when dealing with “new startup tokens,” or NFTs.

### Number 1: NFTs Being Created for Non-Existing Companies
